2. DO NOT re-dispatch, delegate, hand off, spawn sub-agents, or "assign to a reviewer/

# Task-type: code-review-adversarial

Anchor: `.hopper/tasks/code-review-adversarial.md::root`

## Purpose

Find bugs, edge cases, security issues, performance regressions, and design holes in submitted work — WITHOUT fixing them. Output is a findings document; not a code change.

## Input shape

The task receives:
- A target artifact: **PR diff or output.md from a `code-impl` task** (NOT spec docs — those belong to `spec-blindspot-hunt`; boundary tightened per codex Phase 0 audit F4)
- Scope qualifier: "review the diff against base branch" / "review the architecture for race conditions in this implementation"
- Optional focus: security / performance / correctness / API design

## Output shape (output.md schema)

`<task-id>-output.md` (or `critic-<target-id>.md` for backwards compat) MUST contain:

- **Summary**: 1 paragraph stating what was reviewed and what severity profile the findings have
- **Files reviewed**: paths + LOC reviewed
- **Findings (severity-ordered)**: each finding as `[F<N>] <severity P0/P1/P2>: <one-line>` + Root cause (2-3 sentences) + Recommended fix
- **Verdict**: PASS | PASS_WITH_CHANGES | REWORK (consumers of adversarial review may convert REWORK into a follow-up task)
- **Commit**: `<short-sha>` of the review commit itself (the findings doc is the artifact)
- **Checks**: did review touch only the findings doc? (`git diff --name-only` should show only review file + queue.md status flip)
- **Next recommendation**: cursor-aware; if REWORK, suggest the rework task ID

## Acceptance type

**verdict-bearing**. The reviewer's verdict is the primary acceptance signal. Reviewer does NOT need to prove anything — they emit findings + verdict. Consumer of review (Leader / Strategy) decides whether to act.

## Boundary with adjacent task-types

- **vs `code-review-acceptance`**: adversarial finds bugs, doesn't grade against acceptance. Acceptance review grades against pre-written acceptance criteria (accept / accept-with-note / rework / revert).
- **vs `code-impl`**: adversarial review writes a findings doc; code-impl writes product code. Reviewer MUST NOT edit product code.
- **vs `spec-blindspot-hunt`** *(boundary tightened per codex F4)*: **adversarial scope = code / diffs / implementation outputs ONLY. Spec documents and design proposals are handled by `spec-blindspot-hunt`.** If a single dispatch needs both spec-level audit and code-level audit, that's TWO tasks (one of each type), not one.

## Vendor preference

Default: handled out-of-band by Strategy invoking `/codex` GPT-5 xhigh (cross-audit pattern per goal directive 2026-05-20). NOT typically dispatched through queue.md to a vendor adapter, because the dispatcher (Strategy) needs the adversarial findings raw to decide downstream.

If queued via plugin: codex-builder OR claude-opus-via-out-of-band-strategy-invocation (the latter is preferred for "fresh subagent" semantics).

## Anti-persona note

This frame describes TASK SHAPE, not AGENT IDENTITY. Avoid identity-claiming language and role-impersonation phrases in any dispatched prompt. The verb "adversarial" is in the task-type name to signal intent; the vendor doesn't need a costume. Vendor brings its own rigor. Banned-phrase enumeration omitted here to keep the anti-persona grep verifier clean. (Per codex Phase 0 audit F3 fix.)

## Task spec

ADVERSARIAL REVIEW of a root-cause analysis. Your job is to TRY TO REFUTE it, not to confirm it. Default to skepticism: for each claim, state CONFIRMED / OVERSTATED / WRONG / UNVERIFIABLE and say what evidence you actually checked. Reading source and logs is your METHOD; your deliverable is a judgment.

READ-ONLY. Do not modify any file.

=== CONTEXT ===

A user dispatched two read-only review tasks to the `grok` vendor through the hopper-plugin dispatcher, from a ChatGPT-app host chain on Windows. Both were recorded as FAILED with `adapter_status: auth-fail` / `adapter_diagnostic_code: adapter-auth-failed`. The user asked whether this is a usage problem or a design problem. I produced the analysis below. Review it.

  - cli/src/vendors/codex.js         <- has env() isolation; grok does not

=== CLAIMS TO REFUTE ===

C1. BOTH grok runs actually SUCCEEDED; hopper misclassified them.
    Asserted evidence: exit_code 0; duration 196384ms; the raw log ends with a complete
    JSON result envelope that parses cleanly from byte offset 149691, containing
    stopReason "end_turn", a `text` field of 16854 characters, num_turns 5,
    total_cost_usd 0.318382. The second run likewise completed (7612 output tokens).
    => Verify the offset and the envelope yourself. Is "the run succeeded" actually
       supported, or could an end_turn envelope coexist with a genuine auth failure?

C2. DEFECT A - extractGrokText's third candidate anchors on the FIRST "{" in stdout.
    In cli/src/vendors/grok.js, the "framed object" candidate (added in 0.35.1) slices
    from the first "{" to the last "}". grok CLI's own startup WARN line contains
    `ParseFile { path: "C:\Users\litianyi\.cursor\hooks.json", detail: ... }` at offset
    145948, i.e. BEFORE the real envelope. So the slice is garbage and JSON.parse fails,
    leaving parsedJson=false and skipping the success branch.
    => Read the actual function. Is my description of its candidate order correct?
       Is anchoring on the first "{" really the defect, or is something else the
       decisive failure? Would fixing ONLY this make the run succeed?

C3. DEFECT B - hasSpecificGrokAuthFailure is not specific.
    Its regex is:
      /\b(?:unauthorized|invalid(?:\s+(?:api\s*)?key)?|login\s+required|sign[ -]?in\s+required|AuthorizationRequired)\b|\b(?:HTTP\s*)?(?:401|403)\b/i
    I claim `invalid(?:\s+(?:api\s*)?key)?` makes the qualifier OPTIONAL so a bare
    "invalid" matches, and `\b(?:HTTP\s*)?(?:401|403)\b` matches a bare 401/403 anywhere
    (line numbers, byte counts, token counts, ports).
    => Verify against the regex as written. Count the matches in the real log and say
       what text produced them. Is my reading of the alternation/grouping correct?

C4. The two defects are the SAME FAMILY as a bug fixed in 0.47.0, where
    `/not found|command not found/i.test(raw.stderr)` misread a reviewer's prose as
    `adapter-binary-missing`. I claim whoever fixed that one (me) failed to audit the
    neighbouring matchers in the same file.
    => Check grok.js, claude.js, kimi.js, opencode.js for other whole-stream substring
       heuristics. Is the family characterization right? Are there MORE such matchers
       I have not named? Name them with file and line.

C5. grok CLI reads ~/.claude/settings.json, ~/.claude/plugins/**, and ~/.cursor/hooks.json
    BY ITSELF - not because of the ChatGPT app and not because of hopper.
    Asserted evidence: hopper passes `{ ...process.env, ...adapterEnv }` (subprocess.js
    ~216) and the grok adapter defines no env(); running `grok inspect` by hand from a
    plain shell with no hopper involved reports
    "Permissions - Source: C:\Users\litianyi\.claude\settings.json (settings) - 276 loaded,
    22 skipped" and "Skills (238)" including hopper's own skills.
    => Is the inference sound? Could hopper still be causing this some other way
       (cwd, an inherited env var, a config file it writes)? Check.

C6. DESIGN GAP - hopper implements vendor environment isolation for codex ONLY.
    codex.js has env() + resolveIsolatedCodexHome() whose stated purpose is
    "isolate the dispatched codex from the HOST's global config so dispatch stays
    deterministic (Host != Vendor, spec section 3 #4)". No other adapter has env().
    I claim the same rationale applies to grok and that its absence is a design gap,
    with these consequences: the vendor inherits 276 host permission rules, loads 238
    host skills including hopper's own 22, and loads a STALE hopper 0.32.0 clone at
    ~/.claude/plugins/hopper which wins a name collision over the installed 0.48.1.
    => Is "design gap" the right verdict, or is full env inheritance a defensible
       choice? Is there a reason codex is special that also excuses grok? Does the
       vendor loading the dispatcher's own skills create a real recursion/contamination
       risk, or am I overstating it? Is GROK_HOME actually a viable isolation lever?

C7. VERDICT: "design problem, not usage problem."
    Contributing environment problems exist on the user's machine (malformed
    ~/.cursor/hooks.json; a gitnexus MCP binary that is not a valid Win32 executable;
    the stale hopper clone) but grok ran to completion despite all of them, so I claim
    they are triggers, not causes.
    => Is this apportionment correct? Make the case for the opposite reading if one
       exists.

=== WHAT I WANT BACK ===

1. Per-claim verdict table: C1..C7, each CONFIRMED / OVERSTATED / WRONG / UNVERIFIABLE,
   with the specific evidence you checked (file + line, or log offset).
2. Anything I MISSED - especially additional defects in the same family, or a simpler
   explanation of the failure that I have overcomplicated.
3. The single most likely way this analysis is wrong.
4. Whether the proposed fix order is right: (a) add vendor env isolation, (b) fix the
   two heuristics, (c) add a structural rule that a parseable end_turn envelope with
   exit 0 may never be overridden by a substring heuristic. Argue against it if you can.

Be concrete and cite what you read. If you cannot verify something, say UNVERIFIABLE
rather than guessing.

  126:     // Headless = no interactive approval is possible: a background dispatch, an
  127:     // injected log file, or a non-TTY stdout (a host shell driving us). In that
  128:     // case grok needs an explicit permission mode (and, for full-access, also
  129:     // --always-approve) or it stalls and returns stopReason:"Cancelled" with
  130:     // Auth(AuthorizationRequired) + "worker quit" (vendor-preset feedback
  131:     // 2026-06-15 — --always-approve alone was insufficient). bypassPermissions is
  132:     // the headless-safe mode (CONFIRMED `grok --help`:
  133:     // default|acceptEdits|auto|dontAsk|bypassPermissions|plan). The --sandbox opt
  134:     // still gates --always-approve (full-access only). Escape hatch:
  135:     // HOPPER_GROK_PERMISSION_MODE overrides the mode (empty = omit it on grok
  136:     // builds that lack the flag).
  137:     const headless = Boolean(opts.background || opts.logFile || !process.stdout.isTTY);
  138:     const permMode = process.env.HOPPER_GROK_PERMISSION_MODE ?? 'bypassPermissions';
  139:     return [
  140:       '-p', input,
  141:       '--output-format', 'json',
  142:       '--no-auto-update',
  143:       '-m', opts.model ?? DEFAULT_MODEL,
  144:       // Anchor the working dir explicitly (CONFIRMED `--cwd <PATH>` docs.x.ai).
  145:       // hopper injects opts.cwd = resolved vendor CWD (repo root by default, or
  146:       // $HOPPER_VENDOR_CWD). grok's sandbox is relative to --cwd, so a widened
  147:       // root reaches external paths without disabling grok's permission model.
  148:       ...(opts.cwd ? ['--cwd', opts.cwd] : []),
  149:       ...(headless && permMode ? ['--permission-mode', permMode] : []),
  150:       ...(headless && sandbox === 'danger-full-access' ? ['--always-approve'] : []),
  151:       // Reasoning effort. grok --effort vocabulary is low|medium|high, but the
  152:       // dispatch layer now defaults opts.reasoning to the canonical max 'xhigh'
  153:       // (5-level scale), so clamp it down (xhigh→high, minimal→low). Escape hatch:
  154:       // HOPPER_GROK_EFFORT overrides the level (empty string omits --effort
  155:       // entirely, for grok builds that predate the flag). A direct adapter call
  156:       // with no reasoning + no env still emits NO --effort (opt-in preserved).
  157:       ...(() => {
  158:         const raw = process.env.HOPPER_GROK_EFFORT !== undefined
  159:           ? process.env.HOPPER_GROK_EFFORT
  160:           : opts.reasoning;
  161:         const eff = clampGrokEffort(raw);
  162:         return eff ? ['--effort', eff] : [];
  163:       })(),
  164:       ...(opts.conversationId ? ['-r', opts.conversationId] : []),
  165:     ];
  166:   },

  168:   envPreflight() {
  169:     // This stays a zero-spawn, non-secret context check. A browser/OAuth state
  170:     // in another session might not be inherited by the Hopper Node parent, so
  171:     // no local artifact can establish remotely verified authentication.
  172:     const authContext = grokAuthContext();
  173:     const note = authContext === 'key-present-unverified'
  174:       ? 'Note: XAI_API_KEY is present, but this zero-spawn check cannot validate it remotely.'
  175:       : authContext === 'credential-artifact-present-unverified'
  176:         ? 'Note: a Grok credential artifact is present, but this zero-spawn check cannot validate it remotely.'
  177:         : authContext === 'not-detected'
  178:           ? 'Note: no XAI_API_KEY or recognized ~/.grok credential artifact was detected. This zero-spawn check cannot verify remote auth; browser state in another session may not be inherited.'
  179:           : 'Note: Grok credential context could not be read. This zero-spawn check cannot verify remote auth.';
  180:     return {
  181:       ok: true,
  182:       missing: [note],
  183:       authContext,
  184:     };
  185:   },
  186: 
  187:   timeoutMs(opts) {
  188:     // Native: 300s (agentic coding CLI; same tier as codex).
  189:     const native = 300_000;
  190:     // Phase 6c F1: review task-types raised to 30min floor (all-adapter consistent).
  191:     return applyTaskTypeFloor(native, opts);
  192:   },
  193: 
  194:   parseResult(raw) {
  195:     if (raw.timedOut) {
  196:       return adapterFailure('timeout', 'adapter-timeout');
  197:     }
  198:     if (raw.exitCode === 127 || /not found|command not found/i.test(raw.stderr || '')) {
  199:       return adapterFailure('permission-fail', 'adapter-binary-missing');
  200:     }
  201:     const signal = `${raw.stdout || ''}\n${raw.stderr || ''}`;
  202:     const parsed = extractGrokText(raw.stdout);
  203:     const outputEvidence = grokOutputEvidence(parsed);
  204:     const stop = String(parsed.stopReason || '').trim();
  205:     const unsuccessfulStop = /cancel|abort|refus|error|fatal/i.test(stop);
  206:     if (raw.exitCode === 0 && parsed.parsedJson && !parsed.hasError && !unsuccessfulStop && parsed.text.trim()) {
  207:       const result = parsed.usage
  208:         ? { text: parsed.text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none', usage: parsed.usage }
  209:         : { text: parsed.text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none' };
  210:       return outputEvidence ? { ...result, outputEvidence } : result;
  211:     }
  212:     const status = hasSpecificGrokAuthFailure(signal) ? 'auth-fail' : 'unknown-fail';
  213:     const diagnostic = status === 'auth-fail'
  214:       ? 'adapter-auth-failed'
  215:       : (raw.exitCode === 0 ? 'adapter-protocol-invalid' : 'adapter-unknown-failed');
  216:     return failedGrokOutput(status, diagnostic, parsed, outputEvidence);
  217:   },
  218: };
  219: 
  220: /**
  221:  * Clamp the canonical 5-level reasoning scale to grok's known-good --effort
  222:  * vocabulary (low|medium|high). xhigh→high, minimal→low; unknown/empty → null
  223:  * (omit the flag). Exported-by-position next to args() for locality.
  224:  * @param {string|undefined|null} level
  225:  * @returns {string|null}
  226:  */
  227: function clampGrokEffort(level) {
  228:   switch (level) {
  229:     case 'xhigh':
  230:     case 'high': return 'high';
  231:     case 'medium': return 'medium';
  232:     case 'low':
  233:     case 'minimal': return 'low';
  234:     default: return null;
  235:   }
  236: }
  237: 
  238: /**
  239:  * Defensive extraction of answer text from grok --output-format json stdout.
  240:  * Field names are UNCONFIRMED (docs never document the object shape), so try
  241:  * only common fields from a whole or trailing JSON object. Plain stdout is
  242:  * never parser-designated answer text.
  243:  * @param {string} stdout
  244:  * @returns {{ text: string, usage?: object, stopReason?: string, parsedJson: boolean, hasError?: boolean }}
  245:  */
  246: function extractGrokText(stdout) {
  247:   const trimmed = (stdout || '').trim();
  248:   const envelopeKeys = new Set([
  249:     'text', 'content', 'output', 'result', 'response', 'message',
  250:     'stopReason', 'stop_reason', 'finishReason', 'finish_reason', 'error', 'usage',
  251:   ]);
  252:   const noSelectedObject = { text: '', parsedJson: false };
  253:   const fromValue = (value) => {
  254:     const obj = value;
  255:     if (obj && typeof obj === 'object' && !Array.isArray(obj)) {
  256:       const recognized = Object.keys(obj).some((key) => envelopeKeys.has(key));
  257:       if (!recognized) {
  258:         return noSelectedObject;
  259:       }
  260:       const text = obj.text ?? obj.message ?? obj.content ?? obj.output ?? obj.result ?? obj.response;
  261:       const stopReason = obj.stopReason ?? obj.stop_reason ?? obj.finishReason ?? obj.finish_reason;
  262:       return {
  263:         text: typeof text === 'string' ? text : '',
  264:         usage: obj.usage,
  265:         stopReason,
  266:         parsedJson: true,
  267:         hasError: Boolean(obj.error),
  268:       };
  269:     }
  270:     return noSelectedObject;
  271:   };
  272:   const parseCandidate = (candidate) => {
  273:     try {
  274:       return fromValue(JSON.parse(candidate));
  275:     } catch (_) {
  276:       return null;
  277:     }
  278:   };
  279: 
  280:   const whole = parseCandidate(trimmed);
  281:   if (whole) return whole;
  282: 
  283:   // Grok emits one trailing JSON line after warnings. Inspect only that line;
  284:   // earlier unrelated JSON must not hijack result classification.
  285:   const lines = trimmed.split(/\r?\n/).filter((line) => line.trim());
  286:   const trailing = lines[lines.length - 1] || '';
  287:   const tail = trailing === trimmed ? null : parseCandidate(trailing);
  288:   if (tail) return tail;
  289: 
  290:   // Framed multi-line object (ISSUE-grok-adapter-protocol-invalid-false-fail):
  291:   // grok --output-format json pretty-prints the result envelope across MANY
  292:   // lines, and hopper's own runner prepends log lines (e.g. the idle-watchdog
  293:   // notice) to the captured stdout. Neither the whole-stdout parse nor the
  294:   // single trailing line sees the object → false adapter-protocol-invalid on
  295:   // a genuinely successful run. Recover by parsing the span from the FIRST
  296:   // '{' to the LAST '}' — grok's envelope is one top-level object, and the
  297:   // recognized-envelope-keys gate above still rejects a mismatched slice.
  298:   const open = trimmed.indexOf('{');
  299:   const close = trimmed.lastIndexOf('}');
  300:   if (open !== -1 && close > open && (open !== 0 || close !== trimmed.length - 1)) {
  301:     const framed = parseCandidate(trimmed.slice(open, close + 1));
  302:     if (framed) return framed;
  303:   }
  304:   return noSelectedObject;
  305: }
  306: 
  307: function grokOutputEvidence(parsed) {
  308:   if (!parsed?.parsedJson || parsed.hasError || !parsed.text.trim()) return undefined;
  309:   const normalizedStop = String(parsed.stopReason || '').trim();
  310:   return normalizedStop === 'EndTurn'
  311:     ? { completeness: 'verified-complete', source: 'vendor-result-field', terminalMarker: 'grok-end-turn' }
  312:     : { completeness: 'unknown-completeness', source: 'vendor-result-field', terminalMarker: 'none' };
  313: }
  314: 
  315: function hasSpecificGrokAuthFailure(signal) {
  316:   return /\b(?:unauthorized|invalid(?:\s+(?:api\s*)?key)?|login\s+required|sign[ -]?in\s+required|AuthorizationRequired)\b|\b(?:HTTP\s*)?(?:401|403)\b/i.test(signal);
  317: }
  318: 
  319: function failedGrokOutput(status, diagnosticCode, parsed, outputEvidence) {
  320:   const failure = adapterFailure(status, diagnosticCode);
  321:   if (!outputEvidence || !parsed.text.trim()) return failure;
  322:   return { ...failure, text: parsed.text, outputEvidence };
  323: }

 succeeded in 1915ms:

    1: // Codex vendor adapter (T-PLUGIN-05a)
    2: // Anchor: cli/src/vendors/codex.js

    4: // Implements VendorAdapter contract per cli/src/types.js.

    8: import {
    9:   existsSync, mkdirSync, copyFileSync, symlinkSync, lstatSync, readlinkSync, statSync, unlinkSync,
   10:   readFileSync, writeFileSync,
   11: } from 'node:fs';
   12: import { homedir } from 'node:os';
   13: import { join, resolve, sep } from 'node:path';
   14: import { applyTaskTypeFloor } from '../subprocess.js';
   15: 
   16: /**
   17:  * HOPPER-3: build the `-c key=value` overrides that isolate a dispatched codex
   18:  * from the dispatching machine's global config. Without this, `codex exec`
   19:  * loads ~/.codex/config.toml + AGENTS.md (global and project) + marketplace
   20:  * skills + the notify hook from the HOST, which contaminates the vendor run and
   21:  * breaks deterministic dispatch (spec §3 #4, Host != Vendor). On a hopper dev
   22:  * box this is acute: codex would load hopper's OWN marketplace skills while
   23:  * being dispatched BY hopper — a feedback loop.
   24:  *
   25:  * `-c key=value` is the highest-precedence config layer and overrides every
   26:  * config file (verified: developers.openai.com codex config-reference; the
   27:  * precedence table at codex.danielvaughan.com). We override the two keys that
   28:  * `-c` reliably controls:
   29:  *   - project_doc_max_bytes=0 → stop reading AGENTS.md / project + global docs
   30:  *   - notify=[]               → disable the notify hook (a program-argv array)
   31:  *
   32:  * What `-c` CANNOT do: disable globally-installed marketplace skills for a
   33:  * single invocation (openai/codex#20210 — project-scoped skill filtering is
   34:  * unimplemented upstream). Skills are isolated instead by pointing codex at an
   35:  * auto-built, login-preserving CODEX_HOME — see resolveIsolatedCodexHome().
   36:  *
   37:  * Escape hatches (no code change needed):
   38:  *   - HOPPER_CODEX_ISOLATE=0          → disable ALL isolation (these overrides
   39:  *                                        AND the CODEX_HOME swap), e.g. if a
   40:  *                                        future codex changes a key's type and
   41:  *                                        the override would error.
   42:  *   - HOPPER_CODEX_EXTRA_CONFIG="a=b,c=d" → append extra `-c` overrides.
   43:  *
   44:  * @returns {string[]} flat argv fragments, e.g. ['-c', 'project_doc_max_bytes=0', ...]
   45:  */
   46: export function codexIsolationConfig() {
   47:   if (process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_ISOLATE === '0') return [];
   48:   const pairs = ['project_doc_max_bytes=0', 'notify=[]'];
   49:   const extra = process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_EXTRA_CONFIG;
   50:   if (extra) {
   51:     for (const kv of extra.split(',')) {
   52:       const trimmed = kv.trim();
   53:       if (trimmed) pairs.push(trimmed);
   54:     }
   55:   }
   56:   return pairs.flatMap((p) => ['-c', p]);
   57: }
   58: 
   59: /**
   60:  * Disable codex's global orchestration for a dispatched run so the piped brief
   61:  * is the only instruction (ISSUE-codex-callchain-windows). `--disable <feature>`
   62:  * is codex 0.131.0's in-invocation override (= `-c features.<name>=false`; it
   63:  * does NOT write config):
   64:  *   - multi_agent          → no owner/reviewer SUB-AGENT spawns (those sub-spawns
   65:  *                            also hit the Windows CreateProcessWithLogonW 1326)
   66:  *   - hooks / plugin_hooks → no Pre/Post/Stop hook-driven meta-orchestration
   67:  * Without this, global marketplace plugins (e.g. superpowers) re-derive an
   68:  * unrelated task and emit confident OFF-TASK output (a false success).
   69:  * Escape hatch: HOPPER_CODEX_KEEP_ORCHESTRATION=1 keeps codex's defaults.
   70:  * @returns {string[]}
   71:  */
   72: export function codexOrchestrationDisableFlags() {
   73:   if (process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_KEEP_ORCHESTRATION === '1') return [];
   74:   return ['multi_agent', 'hooks', 'plugin_hooks'].flatMap((f) => ['--disable', f]);
   75: }
   76: 
   77: /**
   78:  * HOPPER-3 (auto-isolation): build a cached, login-preserving CODEX_HOME that
   79:  * has the user's auth but NOT their globally-installed marketplace skills, so a
   80:  * dispatched codex runs deterministically with ZERO user setup.
   81:  *
   82:  * codex resolves its home as `$CODEX_HOME || ~/.codex` — a deterministic 1–2
   83:  * candidate lookup (we mirror codex's own rule, not a guess). We discover that
   84:  * real home, then materialize an isolated home (default ~/.hopper/codex-isolated,
   85:  * override via HOPPER_CODEX_HOME) containing:
   86:  *   - auth.json   → symlinked to the real one so token refresh stays live;
   87:  *                   copied as a fallback where symlinks need privilege (Windows).
   88:  *   - config.toml → copied if present (model/provider/MCP config preserved; the
   89:  *                   notify hook is still neutralized by the -c override).
   90:  *   - NO skills/ directory → the host's global marketplace skills do not load.
   91:  *
   92:  * Returns the isolated home path, or null meaning "leave CODEX_HOME as-is"
   93:  * (isolation disabled, no discoverable auth to preserve, or a build failure) —
   94:  * codex then runs against its default home with only the -c overrides applied.
   95:  * Never spawns a subprocess (single-spawn invariant intact).
   96:  *
   97:  * @returns {string|null}
   98:  */
   99: export function resolveIsolatedCodexHome() {
  100:   if (process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_ISOLATE === '0') return null;
  101: 
  102:   const realHome = process.env.CODEX_HOME || join(homedir(), '.codex');
  103:   const realAuth = join(realHome, 'auth.json');
  104:   const haveAuthFile = existsSync(realAuth);
  105:   const haveAuthEnv = Boolean(process.env.CODEX_API_KEY || process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY);
  106:   // Building an isolated home with no auth would strip the user's login. Only
  107:   // isolate when auth can be preserved (a discoverable auth.json, or an env key
  108:   // codex reads directly). Otherwise fall back to the default home + -c.
  109:   if (!haveAuthFile && !haveAuthEnv) return null;
  110: 
  111:   const isoHome = process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_HOME || join(homedir(), '.hopper', 'codex-isolated');
  112:   // Never let the isolated home BE — or live INSIDE — the real home: that would
  113:   // defeat isolation and risk hopper writing/symlinking into the real ~/.codex
  114:   // tree (e.g. a stray HOPPER_CODEX_HOME=~/.codex/sub). Default location is
  115:   // safely outside the real home.
  116:   const isoResolved = resolve(isoHome);
  117:   const realResolved = resolve(realHome);
  118:   if (isoResolved === realResolved || isoResolved.startsWith(realResolved + sep)) return null;
  119: 
  120:   try {
  121:     mkdirSync(isoHome, { recursive: true });
  122:     if (haveAuthFile) linkOrCopy(realAuth, join(isoHome, 'auth.json'));
  123:     const realCfg = join(realHome, 'config.toml');
  124:     // ISSUE-codex-review-hijack: copy config.toml but STRIP skill registrations.
  125:     // The skill-free isoHome/skills already excludes DIRECTORY skills, but
  126:     // config-registered skills ([[skills.config]] absolute paths / a [skills]
  127:     // table) would otherwise be re-introduced by copying the host config — which
  128:     // is exactly how gstack/superpowers/cli-audit skills hijacked dispatched
  129:     // reviews. Sanitizing keeps model/provider/MCP config intact.
  130:     if (existsSync(realCfg)) writeSanitizedCodexConfig(realCfg, join(isoHome, 'config.toml'));
  131:     // Intentionally never create isoHome/skills — that omission IS the isolation.
  132:     return isoHome;
  133:   } catch (_) {
  134:     return null;  // any build error → safe fallback to default home + -c overrides
  135:   }
  136: }
  137: 
  138: /** Symlink src→dest (preferred — keeps token refresh live); copy as fallback. */
  139: function linkOrCopy(src, dest) {
  140:   try {
  141:     if (lstatExists(dest)) {
  142:       try {
  143:         const st = lstatSync(dest);
  144:         if (st.isSymbolicLink() && resolve(readlinkSync(dest)) === resolve(src)) return;
  145:       } catch (_) {}
  146:       try { unlinkSync(dest); } catch (_) {}
  147:     }
  148:     try { symlinkSync(src, dest); return; } catch (_) { /* privilege/unsupported → copy */ }
  149:     copyFileSync(src, dest);
  150:   } catch (_) { /* leave whatever exists; resolveIsolatedCodexHome() will catch */ }
  151: }
  152: 
  153: /** lstat-based existence check that also sees broken symlinks. */
  154: function lstatExists(p) {
  155:   try { lstatSync(p); return true; } catch (_) { return false; }
  156: }
  157: 
  158: /** Copy src→dest only when src is newer than dest (or dest is absent). */
  159: function refreshCopy(src, dest) {
  160:   try {
  161:     if (existsSync(dest)) {
  162:       try { if (statSync(dest).mtimeMs >= statSync(src).mtimeMs) return; } catch (_) {}
  163:     }
  164:     copyFileSync(src, dest);
  165:   } catch (_) { /* best-effort */ }
  166: }
  167: 
  168: /**
  169:  * Strip host orchestration from a codex config.toml so a dispatched, isolated
  170:  * codex runs ONLY the piped brief (ISSUE-codex-review-hijack +
  171:  * ISSUE-codex-callchain-windows). Removes the `[skills...]`, `[plugins...]`,
  172:  * `[marketplaces...]`, and `[[hooks...]]`/`[hooks...]` tables (plus their bodies,
  173:  * up to the next table header). codex 0.131.0 moved global skills to marketplace
  174:  * PLUGINS (e.g. `[plugins."superpowers@openai-curated"]`) and drives
  175:  * meta-orchestration via Pre/Post/Stop hooks — the old skills-only strip missed
  176:  * both, so dispatched reviews kept getting hijacked. Leaves model/provider/MCP/
  177:  * sandbox/auth config untouched. Pure — exported for unit testing.
  178:  * @param {string} toml
  179:  * @returns {string}
  180:  */
  181: export function stripCodexSkillsConfig(toml) {
  182:   const lines = String(toml ?? '').split(/\r?\n/);
  183:   const isTableHeader = (l) => /^\s*\[\[?[^\]]+\]\]?\s*(#.*)?$/.test(l);
  184:   // codex 0.131.0 hijacks the brief via marketplace plugins (e.g.
  185:   // [plugins."superpowers@openai-curated"]) + [marketplaces.*] + Pre/Post/Stop
  186:   // [[hooks.*]] — none of which the old skills-only strip removed.
  187:   const isHijackHeader = (l) => /^\s*\[\[?\s*(skills|plugins|marketplaces|hooks)(\.|\s|\]|\b)/i.test(l);
  188:   const out = [];
  189:   let skipping = false;
  190:   for (const line of lines) {
  191:     if (isTableHeader(line)) {
  192:       skipping = isHijackHeader(line);
  193:       if (skipping) continue;          // drop the hijack table header itself
  194:     }
  195:     if (!skipping) out.push(line);     // keep preamble + model/provider/MCP/sandbox + bodies
  196:   }
  197:   return out.join('\n');
  198: }
  199: 
  200: /** Read src config, strip skill registrations, write to dest (idempotent). */
  201: function writeSanitizedCodexConfig(src, dest) {
  202:   try {
  203:     const cleaned = stripCodexSkillsConfig(readFileSync(src, 'utf-8'));
  204:     if (existsSync(dest)) {
  205:       try { if (readFileSync(dest, 'utf-8') === cleaned) return; } catch (_) {}
  206:     }
  207:     writeFileSync(dest, cleaned, 'utf-8');
  208:   } catch (_) { /* best-effort; leave whatever exists */ }
  209: }
  210: 
  211: /**
  212:  * Whether codex should bypass its own sandbox harness
  213:  * (`--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`) instead of honoring the
  214:  * requested `-s <mode>` argv. Platform-split (2026-07-31 reversal of the
  215:  * 2026-06-25 "codex has NO read-only scenario" decision):
  216:  *
  217:  *   - **Windows**: codex's `-s` sandbox harness cannot spawn ANY child process
  218:  *     (`CreateProcessWithLogonW` fails with 1326 — ISSUE-codex-callchain-windows
  219:  *     / ISSUE-codex-windows-sandbox-1326), so bypass stays the DEFAULT there.
  220:  *     `HOPPER_CODEX_SANDBOX_BYPASS=0` is the escape hatch that turns bypass OFF
  221:  *     (falls through to a real, broken `-s` sandbox — for testing only).
  222:  *   - **macOS / Linux**: codex's own `-s <mode>` sandbox is VERIFIED WORKING —
  223:  *     manually verified 2026-07-31: `codex exec -s read-only ... "echo x >
  224:  *     f"` denies the write (`operation not permitted`, file never created),
  225:  *     while `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` with the same command
  226:  *     creates the file. Honoring `-s <mode>` is now the DEFAULT there.
  227:  *     `HOPPER_CODEX_SANDBOX_BYPASS=1` is the escape hatch that turns bypass ON
  228:  *     (opts IN to the old always-full-access-no-OS-sandbox behavior).
  229:  *
  230:  * THE ESCAPE HATCH'S DEFAULT MEANING IS REVERSED BETWEEN THE TWO BRANCHES —
  231:  * same env var, opposite polarity. On Windows, `=0` DISABLES bypass (dangerous:
  232:  * re-enables the sandbox harness that cannot spawn children). On macOS/Linux,
  233:  * `=1` ENABLES bypass (drops back to no-OS-sandbox-at-all). Do not assume "=0
  234:  * always means off"; the meaning depends on which platform is running.
  235:  *
  236:  * @param {NodeJS.Platform} [platform] injectable for tests (defaults to the
  237:  *   real host platform; production callers never override this)
  238:  * @returns {boolean}
  239:  */
  240: export function codexSandboxBypassActive(platform = process.platform) {
  241:   return platform === 'win32'
  242:     ? process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_SANDBOX_BYPASS !== '0'
  243:     : process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_SANDBOX_BYPASS === '1';
  244: }
  245: 
  246: /** @type {import('../types.js').VendorAdapter} */
  247: export const codexAdapter = {
  248:   name: 'codex',
  249:   command: 'codex',
  250:   stdinMode: 'none',
  251:   // Prompt-delivery capability (Windows cmd.exe-shim multi-line truncation fix).
  252:   // `codex exec -` reads the full prompt from stdin (help + docs + repro confirmed),
  253:   // which survives the cmd.exe `.cmd` shim that truncates a multi-line argv positional.
  254:   // The delivery layer routes to stdin ONLY on the win-cmd-shim regime; argv elsewhere.
  255:   // Default ON (proven); env opt-out HOPPER_CODEX_STDIN=0.
  256:   promptStdin: 'supported',
  257:   promptStdinDefault: true,
  258: 
  259:   // Phase 6a static capability hint (no live vendor introspection — would
  260:   // break single-spawn proof). Source: docs/research/.
  261:   capabilities: {
  262:     modelArg: {
  263:       accepted: 'freeform',
  264:       // ISSUE-codex-vendor-model-effort (2026-06): adapter now forwards opts.model
  265:       // as `-m <MODEL>`. ChatGPT-account auth accepts BARE names only — provider-
  266:       // prefixed ids (openai-codex/gpt-5.1-codex) are rejected (openai/codex#12295).
  267:       // Catalog is subscription-dependent; `hopper-dispatch --probe codex`
  268:       // reconciles `codex debug models` with Codex's account-aware cache.
  269:       // gpt-5.6-sol / gpt-5.6-terra / gpt-5.6-luna: V-verified 2026-07-17 via live
  270:       // micro-test on codex CLI 0.144.5; requires codex CLI >= 0.144 (older CLIs 400
  271:       // with 'requires a newer version of Codex' — observed live on 0.142.5). This is
  272:       // exactly the --check-model catalog-only/verified distinction in practice: the
  273:       // bundled catalog listed these three on 0.142.5 too, but they 400'd there — only
  274:       // promote a model out of catalog-only and into knownGood after a live dispatch

  283:       // without hard-coding a model string). gpt-5.6-sol leads for that reason.

  297:     },
