  315:   args(input, opts) {
  316:     const sandbox = opts.sandbox ?? 'danger-full-access';
  317:     // 2026-07-31 platform split (reverses the 2026-06-25 "codex has NO read-only
  318:     // scenario" decision) — see codexSandboxBypassActive() jsdoc for the full
  319:     // rationale + verified evidence. `opts.platform` is a test-only override
  320:     // (real dispatch always uses the host's actual process.platform).
  321:     const platform = opts.platform ?? process.platform;
  322:     const bypassSandbox = codexSandboxBypassActive(platform);
  323:     const sandboxArgs = bypassSandbox
  324:       ? ['--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox']
  325:       : ['-s', sandbox];
  326:     // ISSUE-codex-bypass-flag-missing-from-argv (run #1 footgun): when the vendor
  327:     // CWD is widened to a non-git root (HOPPER_VENDOR_CWD), `codex exec` aborts
  328:     // early with "Not inside a trusted directory and --skip-git-repo-check was not
  329:     // specified" — codex never runs. Manually verified 2026-07-31: this trust gate
  330:     // fires for a REAL `-s read-only` sandbox exactly the same way it fires for the
  331:     // full-access bypass path (same error, same non-git-dir trigger) — it is not
  332:     // specific to bypass mode. So `--skip-git-repo-check` now rides along on EVERY
  333:     // mode, not just the bypass path (`--skip-git-repo-check` is a documented
  334:     // `codex exec` flag; it only affects the trust gate, not sandbox enforcement —
  335:     // a real `-s read-only` invocation still denies writes with the flag present,
  336:     // verified in the same test). Escape hatch: HOPPER_CODEX_SKIP_GIT_CHECK=0 keeps
  337:     // codex's default trust-gate behavior on every mode.
  338:     const skipGitArgs = process.env.HOPPER_CODEX_SKIP_GIT_CHECK !== '0'
  339:       ? ['--skip-git-repo-check']
  340:       : [];
  341:     return [
  342:       // Web search (research/PRD/market dispatches). `--search` opts into LIVE
  343:       // web search (web_search=live); codex enables CACHED search by default, so
  344:       // the old `--enable web_search_cached` was a deprecated no-op (2026 vendor
  345:       // research).
  346:       //
  347:       // POSITION IS LOAD-BEARING: `--search` is a TOP-LEVEL codex flag, accepted
  348:       // only BEFORE the subcommand. Placed after `exec` (where it sat until
  349:       // 0.37.0) codex aborts on argv with `error: unexpected argument '--search'
  350:       // found` and the dispatch never starts -- so every prd-research /
  351:       // market-research task routed to codex died before doing any work, because
  352:       // those task-types auto-enable webSearch. Verified live on codex-cli
  353:       // 0.145.0: `codex exec --search <prompt>` -> unexpected argument;
  354:       // `codex --search exec <prompt>` -> rc=0 with real output; `codex exec
  355:       // --help` does not list it while `codex --help` does.
  356:       //
  357:       // The pre-0.37.0 test asserted only that '--search' appeared SOMEWHERE in
  358:       // argv, which it always did -- presence was never the defect. Its
  359:       // replacement asserts the index, and asserts no other top-level-only flag
  360:       // drifts behind the subcommand.
  361:       ...(opts.webSearch ? ['--search'] : []),
  362:       'exec',
  363:       // Forward an explicit model when the dispatch sets one. `codex exec -m <MODEL>`
  364:       // (ISSUE-codex-vendor-model-effort). ChatGPT-account auth accepts BARE names
  365:       // only (gpt-5.5 / gpt-5.4-mini / gpt-5.3-codex-spark); provider-prefixed ids
  366:       // are rejected (openai/codex#12295). Omitted -> codex account default. Effort
  367:       // is a SEPARATE knob (--reasoning -> model_reasoning_effort), NOT the model name.
  368:       ...(opts.model ? ['-m', opts.model] : []),
  369:       // Set the workspace root explicitly (CONFIRMED `--cd/-C <path>` works with
  370:       // `codex exec`, developers.openai.com/codex/cli/reference). hopper injects
  371:       // opts.cwd = resolved vendor CWD (repo root by default, or $HOPPER_VENDOR_CWD).
  372:       ...(opts.cwd ? ['--cd', opts.cwd] : []),
  373:       ...sandboxArgs,
  374:       ...skipGitArgs,
  375:       '-c', `model_reasoning_effort="${opts.reasoning ?? 'medium'}"`,
  376:       // Suppress codex's global orchestration (multi-agent sub-spawns + hooks) so
  377:       // only the dispatched brief runs — prevents the marketplace-plugin hijack
  378:       // and the 1326 sub-spawn failures (ISSUE-codex-callchain-windows).
  379:       ...codexOrchestrationDisableFlags(),
  380:       // HOPPER-3: isolate the dispatched codex from the HOST's global config so
  381:       // dispatch stays deterministic (Host != Vendor, spec §3 #4).
  382:       ...codexIsolationConfig(),
  383:       // ISSUE-codex-bypass-flag-missing-from-argv (ROOT CAUSE): the PROMPT
  384:       // positional MUST be the LAST argv element. On Windows `codex` is reached
  385:       // through a cmd.exe `.cmd` shim whose command line is capped at ~8191 chars;
  386:       // an over-long line is silently truncated. The composed prompt is large
  387:       // (frame + governance + spec), so with the prompt placed BEFORE the flags
  388:       // (the previous order) the truncation casualty was the TRAILING sandbox /
  389:       // bypass / -c / --disable flags — codex then fell back to its default
  390:       // `workspace-write` sandbox and hit CreateProcessWithLogonW 1326 on every
  391:       // child (a silent no-op). Keeping the prompt last makes the *end of the
  392:       // prompt* the only thing a truncation can eat — the safety flags always
  393:       // reach codex. This also matches codex's own documented usage form,
  394:       // `codex exec [FLAGS] "<prompt>"` (docs/research/async-execution/01-openai-hosts.md).
  395:       // STDIN MODE: when the delivery layer pipes the prompt to stdin (win-cmd-shim,
  396:       // where a multi-line argv positional truncates at the first newline), emit the
  397:       // `-` sentinel so `codex exec … -` reads the FULL prompt from stdin instead.
  398:       ...(opts.promptViaStdin ? ['-'] : [input]),
  399:     ];
  400:   },
  401: 
  402:   // HOPPER-3 (auto-isolation): extra env merged into the codex spawn. Points
  403:   // codex at an auto-built, login-preserving CODEX_HOME with the host's global
  404:   // marketplace skills excluded — zero user setup. Returns {} (no override) when
  405:   // isolation is off or no auth is discoverable, so codex falls back to its
  406:   // default home. Threaded into the spawn by dispatch.js + hopper-runner.
  407:   env() {
  408:     const iso = resolveIsolatedCodexHome();
  409:     return iso ? { CODEX_HOME: iso } : {};
  410:   },
  411: 
  412:   envPreflight() {
  413:     // Per codex Phase 2 audit F1: broaden checks to avoid false-negatives.
  414:     // codex supports: ~/.codex/auth.json (default), $CODEX_HOME override,
  415:     // $CODEX_API_KEY env, $OPENAI_API_KEY env (keychain backed in some installs).
  416:     const codexHome = process.env.CODEX_HOME || join(homedir(), '.codex');
  417:     if (existsSync(join(codexHome, 'auth.json'))) return { ok: true, missing: [] };
  418:     if (process.env.CODEX_API_KEY || process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY) return { ok: true, missing: [] };
  419:     // Soft warn instead of hard block — codex may use keychain we cannot detect.
  420:     return {
  421:       ok: true,
  422:       missing: ['Note: no obvious codex auth artifact found. If smoke fails, run `codex login` OR set CODEX_API_KEY/OPENAI_API_KEY.'],
  423:     };
  424:   },
  425: 
  426:   timeoutMs(opts) {
  427:     // Native: codex scales with reasoning level
  428:     let native = 300_000;
  429:     if (opts.reasoning === 'xhigh') native = 900_000;
  430:     else if (opts.reasoning === 'high') native = 600_000;
  431:     // Phase 6c F1: review task-types get raised to 30min floor
  432:     return applyTaskTypeFloor(native, opts);
  433:   },
  434: 
  435:   parseResult(raw) {
  436:     if (raw.timedOut) {
  437:       return {
  438:         text: raw.stdout,
  439:         status: 'timeout',
  440:         error: `codex exec timed out after ${raw.durationMs}ms`,
  441:       };
  442:     }
  443:     if (raw.exitCode === 127) {
  444:       return {
  445:         text: '',
  446:         status: 'permission-fail',
  447:         error: 'codex binary not found in PATH. Install: see https://github.com/openai/codex',
  448:       };
  449:     }
  450:     // Windows sandbox false-success guard (ISSUE-codex-callchain-windows): codex
  451:     // can exit 0 with confident-looking output while EVERY shell command it ran
  452:     // failed with `CreateProcessWithLogonW failed: 1326` (its sandbox cannot spawn
  453:     // children on this host) — so the dispatched brief was never performed. Surface
  454:     // that as a failure instead of a false `success`.
  455:     //
  456:     // BUT only when codex made NO successful progress. If codex ran ANY command
  457:     // successfully (a `succeeded in <N>ms` / `exited 0 in <N>ms` marker) it was NOT
  458:     // sandbox-blocked, and the 1326 string is either an exec it rerouted around OR —
  459:     // commonly — text codex READ or QUOTED from a file/log it was investigating.
  460:     // Failing those is a false-positive (ISSUE-codex-1326-false-positive: a complete
  461:     // research run that quoted a prior failed run's log was mislabeled permission-fail
  462:     // despite 70 successful commands). codex now always runs full-access (no -s
  463:     // sandbox), so a TOTAL 1326 wipeout should only occur on the HOPPER_CODEX_SANDBOX_BYPASS=0
  464:     // escape-hatch path; this gate keeps the guard as defense-in-depth for that case.
  465:     const combined = `${raw.stdout || ''}\n${raw.stderr || ''}`;
  466:     const sawSandbox1326 = /CreateProcessWithLogonW failed:\s*1326|windows sandbox: CreateProcess\w* failed/i.test(combined);
  467:     const ranAnyCommandOk = /\bsucceeded in \d+\s*ms\b|\bexited 0 in \d+\s*ms\b/i.test(combined);
  468:     if (sawSandbox1326 && !ranAnyCommandOk) {
  469:       return {
  470:         text: raw.stdout,
  471:         status: 'permission-fail',
  472:         error: 'codex could not execute commands: Windows sandbox CreateProcessWithLogonW failed (1326) on every command (no command succeeded). The dispatched brief was likely NOT performed (false success). codex runs full-access (no sandbox) by default; if you forced HOPPER_CODEX_SANDBOX_BYPASS=0, run codex where its sandbox can spawn children.',
  473:       };
  474:     }
  475:     if (raw.exitCode === 0 && raw.stdout) {
  476:       // Parse tokens from stderr metadata if present
  477:       const tokenMatch = raw.stderr.match(/tokens used\s*\n(\d+)/);
  478:       const tokens = tokenMatch ? parseInt(tokenMatch[1]) : undefined;
  479:       return {
  480:         text: raw.stdout.trim(),
  481:         status: 'success',
  482:         usage: tokens ? { totalTokens: tokens } : undefined,
  483:       };
  484:     }
  485:     return {
  486:       text: raw.stdout,
  487:       status: 'unknown-fail',
  488:       error: `codex exited ${raw.exitCode}: ${(raw.stderr || '').slice(0, 500)}`,
  489:     };
  490:   },
  491: };
FILE cli/src/subprocess.js
    1: // Shared subprocess wrapper (T-PLUGIN-04.5)
    2: // Anchor: cli/src/subprocess.js
    3: //
    4: // Per spec v2.0.3 §3 #4 (no harness reaction core):
    5: //   ONE dispatch = ONE selected vendor = ONE subprocess spawn attempt = success OR specific failure.
    6: //   No retry. No fallback. No backoff. No circuit breaker.
    7: //
    8: // Per codex v2.0.3 audit F3 (subprocess kill strategy):
    9: //   Windows: taskkill /PID <pid> /T /F  (kill tree, force)
   10: //   Unix:    spawn detached + process.kill(-pid, 'SIGKILL')  (negative PID = process group)
   11: //   NOT by port — children may be unrelated.
   12: 
   13: import { spawn, execSync } from 'node:child_process';
   14: import { existsSync, readFileSync, readlinkSync, statSync, unlinkSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
   15: import { prepareSubjectRootGuard, wrapSubjectRootInvocation } from './subject-root-guard.js';
   16: 
   17: // Phase 6c F1: task-type-aware timeout floors.
   18: // Adapter-native timeoutMs is tuned for code-impl (short tasks). Review tasks
   19: // inherently need 30+ min because the reviewer reads dozens of files, runs the
   20: // test suite, then writes findings. Without this floor, the 5-vendor dogfood
   21: // audit on 2026-05-21 had all 5 vendors timeout before writing a verdict.
   22: // See docs/audit/phase-6b-dogfood-5vendor.md for the meta-finding.
   23: const REVIEW_TASK_TYPES = new Set([
   24:   'code-review-adversarial',
   25:   'code-review-acceptance',
   26: ]);
   27: const REVIEW_TASK_FLOOR_MS = 1_800_000;  // 30 min floor for any review task
   28: const SERIAL_VENDOR_LOCK_POLL_MS = 250;
   29: const SERIAL_VENDOR_LOCK_STALE_MS = 3_600_000;
   30: const SERIALIZED_VENDORS = new Set(['codex']);
   31: 
   32: /**
   33:  * Apply task-type-aware floor to an adapter-native timeout.
   34:  * - For review task-types: returns max(native, REVIEW_TASK_FLOOR_MS).
   35:  *   Lets a vendor like codex with reasoning=xhigh still extend beyond the
   36:  *   floor if it wants more time; capped vendors get raised TO the floor.
   37:  * - For non-review task-types: returns native unchanged.
   38:  *
   39:  * Adapters call this from their `timeoutMs(opts)` implementation. The
   40:  * dispatch/runner path is responsible for setting `opts.taskType`.
   41:  */
   42: export function applyTaskTypeFloor(nativeMs, opts) {
   43:   if (!opts || !opts.taskType) return nativeMs;
   44:   if (REVIEW_TASK_TYPES.has(opts.taskType)) {
   45:     return Math.max(nativeMs, REVIEW_TASK_FLOOR_MS);
   46:   }
   47:   return nativeMs;
   48: }
   49: 
   50: export { REVIEW_TASK_TYPES, REVIEW_TASK_FLOOR_MS };
   51: 
   52: // ── idle + ceiling timeout primitive (replaces the single total-wall-clock cap) ──
   53: // The old design hard-killed at one per-vendor TOTAL timeout, which could not
   54: // tell "hung" from "legitimately working on a long task" — see
   55: // ISSUE-mimo-codeimpl-timeout (a 41-edit code-impl killed at the flat 180s).
   56: // New design (two timers):
   57: //   - idle timeout: kill ONLY after idleMs of ZERO output (stdout+stderr) — the
   58: //     real "is it stuck" detector. An actively-streaming long task keeps
   59: //     resetting it and is NOT killed (most agentic vendors stream json / log /
   60: //     tool-call events under hopper's --format json / --print-logs flags).
   61: //   - absolute ceiling: a generous safety net so a runaway can't live forever.
   62: // Both env-overridable; an explicit per-task --timeout sets the ceiling.
   63: const DEFAULT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS = 180_000;   // 3 min of silence ⇒ treat as stuck
   64: const CEILING_FLOOR_MS = 1_800_000;        // ≥30 min absolute ceiling (safety net)
   65: const WINDOWS_TIMEOUT_CLOSE_GRACE_MS = 250;
   66: 
   67: function envPositiveInt(name) {
   68:   const v = Number.parseInt(process.env[name] ?? '', 10);
   69:   return Number.isFinite(v) && v > 0 ? v : null;
   70: }
   71: 
   72: /**
   73:  * Resolve {idleMs, ceilingMs} for a dispatch from the adapter baseline + env +
   74:  * an optional explicit per-task override (--timeout → opts.timeoutOverrideMs).
   75:  *
   76:  * Ceiling precedence: explicit --timeout > HOPPER_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS >
   77:  *   max(adapter baseline, CEILING_FLOOR_MS).
   78:  * Idle: HOPPER_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS > DEFAULT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS, never above ceiling.
   79:  *
   80:  * @param {number} adapterBaselineMs  adapter.timeoutMs(opts) (per-vendor tuned)
   81:  * @param {object} [opts]             may carry timeoutOverrideMs (ms)
   82:  * @returns {{ idleMs: number, ceilingMs: number }}
   83:  */
   84: export function resolveDispatchTimeouts(adapterBaselineMs, opts = {}) {
   85:   const idleMs = envPositiveInt('HOPPER_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS') ?? DEFAULT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS;
   86:   const override = Number.isFinite(opts?.timeoutOverrideMs) && opts.timeoutOverrideMs > 0
   87:     ? opts.timeoutOverrideMs
   88:     : null;
   89:   const ceilingMs = override
   90:     ?? envPositiveInt('HOPPER_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS')
   91:     ?? Math.max(Number(adapterBaselineMs) || 0, CEILING_FLOOR_MS);
   92:   return { idleMs: Math.min(idleMs, ceilingMs), ceilingMs };
   93: }
   94: 
   95: export { DEFAULT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS, CEILING_FLOOR_MS, WINDOWS_TIMEOUT_CLOSE_GRACE_MS };
   96: 
   97: /**
   98:  * Idle-detection helper for vendors that emit a periodic heartbeat to their log even when
   99:  * otherwise idle (e.g. mimo's `--print-logs` GET /session/status poll). Raw log-size growth
  100:  * never goes quiet for those, so the idle timer's "no growth = stuck" detector never fires and
  101:  * a delivered-but-non-exiting process (the mimo background-exit hang) waits out the full
  102:  * ceiling. Given a freshly-appended log chunk and a heartbeat matcher, returns true iff the
  103:  * chunk contains at least one NON-empty line that is NOT a heartbeat line (i.e. real progress).
  104:  * The runner resets the idle clock only on such substantive growth. Pure; exported for tests.
  105:  * @param {string} chunk         newly-appended log bytes (decoded)
  106:  * @param {RegExp} heartbeatRe   matches a heartbeat (noise) line; non-global
  107:  * @returns {boolean}
  108:  */
  109: export function chunkHasSubstantiveLine(chunk, heartbeatRe) {
  110:   if (!chunk) return false;
  111:   for (const line of chunk.split(/\r?\n/)) {
  112:     const t = line.trim();
  113:     if (!t) continue;
  114:     if (heartbeatRe && heartbeatRe.test(t)) continue;
  115:     return true;
  116:   }
  117:   return false;
  118: }
  119: 
  120: import { platform, tmpdir } from 'node:os';
  121: import { join } from 'node:path';
  122: 
  123: /**
  124:  * Serialize selected vendors across processes when the upstream CLI cannot
  125:  * safely share auth/session state concurrently. Current scope: codex only.
  126:  *
  127:  * @param {string | undefined} vendorName
  128:  * @returns {Promise<() => void>} release callback
  129:  */
  130: export async function acquireVendorLock(vendorName) {
  131:   if (!vendorName || !SERIALIZED_VENDORS.has(vendorName)) {
  132:     return () => {};
  133:   }
  134: 
  135:   const lockPath = join(tmpdir(), `hopper-${vendorName}.lock`);
  136:   const lockToken = `${process.pid}:${Date.now()}:${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 10)}`;
  137: 
  138:   while (true) {
  139:     try {
  140:       writeFileSync(lockPath, lockToken, { flag: 'wx' });
  141:       return () => releaseVendorLock(lockPath, lockToken);
  142:     } catch (err) {
  143:       if (err.code !== 'EEXIST') throw err;
  144:       if (isVendorLockStale(lockPath)) {
  145:         try {
  146:           unlinkSync(lockPath);
  147:           continue;
  148:         } catch (_) {
  149:           // Another process may have reclaimed it first.
  150:         }
  151:       }
  152:       await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, SERIAL_VENDOR_LOCK_POLL_MS));
  153:     }
  154:   }
  155: }
  156: 
  157: function releaseVendorLock(lockPath, lockToken) {
  158:   try {
  159:     if (!existsSync(lockPath)) return;
  160:     const current = readFileSync(lockPath, 'utf-8');
  161:     if (current === lockToken) unlinkSync(lockPath);
  162:   } catch (_) {
  163:     // best-effort; stale lock recovery handles crash leftovers
  164:   }
  165: }
  166: 
  167: function isVendorLockStale(lockPath) {
  168:   try {
  169:     return (Date.now() - statSync(lockPath).mtimeMs) > SERIAL_VENDOR_LOCK_STALE_MS;
  170:   } catch (_) {
  171:     return false;
  172:   }
  173: }
  174: 
  175: /**
  176:  * Run a vendor subprocess EXACTLY ONCE with hard timeout + process-tree kill.
  177:  *
  178:  * @param {object} args
  179:  * @param {string} args.command          CLI command (e.g. "codex", "kimi", "agy")
  180:  * @param {string[]} args.args           Argv array
  181:  * @param {string|null} args.stdinInput  Input to pipe to stdin (null = none)
  182:  * @param {number} args.timeoutMs        Hard timeout
  183:  * @param {object} [args.env]            Extra env vars (merged with process.env)
  184:  * @param {string} [args.cwd]            Working directory
  185:  * @param {string|null} [args.logFilePath]  Path to vendor --log-file (read after exit if set)
  186:  * @param {string} [args.vendorName]     Logical vendor name for serialization guard
  187:  * @param {string} [args.subjectRoot]     Explicit read-only subject tree, if requested
  188:  * @param {string} [args.sandbox]         Effective sandbox used to validate subjectRoot
  189:  * @param {object} [args.subjectGuardOptions] Test-only dependency overrides for guard validation
  190:  * @returns {Promise<import('./types.js').SubprocessResult>}
  191:  */
  192: export async function runSubprocessOnce({
  193:   command,
  194:   args,
  195:   stdinInput,
  196:   timeoutMs,
  197:   idleMs = 0,
  198:   env,
  199:   cwd,
  200:   logFilePath = null,
  201:   vendorName,
  202:   subjectRoot = null,
  203:   sandbox,
  204:   subjectGuardOptions,
  205: }) {
  206:   const isWindows = platform() === 'win32';
  207:   const startedAt = Date.now();
  208:   // Validation/backend availability is deliberately before the vendor spawn and
  209:   // before lock acquisition: a requested guard is mandatory, never advisory.
  210:   const subjectGuard = prepareSubjectRootGuard({ subjectRoot, sandbox, ...(subjectGuardOptions || {}) });
  211:   const invocation = wrapSubjectRootInvocation(command, args, subjectGuard);
  212:   const releaseVendorLock = await acquireVendorLock(vendorName);
  213: 
  214:   try {
  215:     const child = spawn(invocation.command, invocation.args, {
  216:       env: { ...process.env, ...(env || {}) },
  217:       cwd: cwd || process.cwd(),
  218:       stdio: [stdinInput == null ? 'ignore' : 'pipe', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  219:       detached: !isWindows,                                       // Unix: detached process group for tree-kill
  220:       windowsHide: isWindows,
  221:     });
  222: 
  223:     let stdout = '';
  224:     let stderr = '';
  225:     let timedOut = false;
  226:     let timeoutReason = null;     // 'idle' | 'ceiling' (diagnostic)
  227:     let processCleanup = { status: 'not-needed', method: null };
  228:     let killTimer = null;
  229:     let idleTimer = null;
  230:     let windowsCleanupTimer = null;
  231:     let settleExit = null;
  232:     let exitSettled = false;
  233: 
  234:     const clearTimeoutTimers = () => {
  235:       if (killTimer) clearTimeout(killTimer);
  236:       if (idleTimer) clearTimeout(idleTimer);
  237:       if (windowsCleanupTimer) clearTimeout(windowsCleanupTimer);
  238:       killTimer = null;
  239:       idleTimer = null;
  240:       windowsCleanupTimer = null;
  241:     };
  242: 
  243:     const settleWindowsCleanupFallback = () => {
  244:       if (!isWindows || !timedOut || exitSettled) return;
  245:       // `taskkill /T /F` is synchronous, but a Windows ChildProcess can still
  246:       // omit its `close` event after that tree has been reaped. Ask the owned
  247:       // direct child to terminate too, then settle from the bounded cleanup
  248:       // path if its streams never close. This is cleanup only, never a second
  249:       // vendor invocation or dispatch retry.
  250:       if (child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null) {
  251:         try { child.kill('SIGKILL'); } catch (_) {}
  252:       }
  253:       windowsCleanupTimer = setTimeout(() => {
  254:         if (!exitSettled) {
  255:           try { child.stdin?.destroy(); } catch (_) {}
  256:           try { child.stdout?.destroy(); } catch (_) {}
  257:           try { child.stderr?.destroy(); } catch (_) {}
  258:           settleExit?.(child.exitCode, child.signalCode || 'SIGKILL');
  259:         }
  260:       }, WINDOWS_TIMEOUT_CLOSE_GRACE_MS);
  261:     };
  262: 
  263:     const armWindowsCleanupFallback = () => {
  264:       if (!isWindows || windowsCleanupTimer || exitSettled) return;
  265:       windowsCleanupTimer = setTimeout(settleWindowsCleanupFallback, WINDOWS_TIMEOUT_CLOSE_GRACE_MS);
  266:     };
  267: 
  268:     const claimTimeout = (reason) => {
  269:       if (timedOut) return false;
  270:       timedOut = true;
  271:       timeoutReason = reason;
  272:       // Clear the competing source before the synchronous tree-kill. Output
  273:       // arriving after a best-effort cleanup must not re-arm idle either.
  274:       clearTimeoutTimers();
  275:       processCleanup = killProcessTree(child.pid, isWindows);
  276:       armWindowsCleanupFallback();
  277:       return true;
  278:     };
  279: 
  280:     // idle timer — resets on every chunk of vendor output; fires only after
  281:     // idleMs of total silence (hung / reverse-pressured). ADDITIVE: when idleMs
  282:     // is falsy this is a no-op, so callers/tests passing only timeoutMs keep the
  283:     // legacy single-ceiling behavior unchanged.
  284:     const armIdle = () => {
  285:       if (!(idleMs > 0) || timedOut) return;
  286:       if (idleTimer) clearTimeout(idleTimer);
  287:       idleTimer = setTimeout(() => claimTimeout('idle'), idleMs);
  288:       if (idleTimer.unref) idleTimer.unref();
  289:     };
  290: 
  291:     child.stdout.on('data', (chunk) => { stdout += chunk.toString(); armIdle(); });
  292:     child.stderr.on('data', (chunk) => { stderr += chunk.toString(); armIdle(); });
  293: 
  294:     if (stdinInput != null && child.stdin) {
  295:       try {
  296:         child.stdin.write(stdinInput);
  297:         child.stdin.end();
  298:       } catch (_) {
  299:         // child may have exited before stdin ready — ignore; exit code captures result
  300:       }
  301:     }
  302: 
  303:     // Absolute ceiling. NO retry on timeout — timeout = failure, surface it.
  304:     if (timeoutMs > 0) {
  305:       killTimer = setTimeout(() => claimTimeout('ceiling'), timeoutMs);
  306:     }
  307:     armIdle();  // start the idle clock at spawn (silence before first byte counts)
  308: 
  309:     const exitCode = await new Promise((resolve) => {
  310:       settleExit = (code, signal) => {
  311:         if (exitSettled) return;
  312:         exitSettled = true;
  313:         clearTimeoutTimers();
  314:         // Map signal to exit-code-equivalent (POSIX convention: 128 + signal)
  315:         resolve(code != null ? code : 128 + (signal === 'SIGKILL' ? 9 : signal === 'SIGTERM' ? 15 : 0));
  316:       };
  317:       child.on('close', settleExit);
  318:       child.on('error', (_err) => settleExit(127, null)); // command-not-found convention
  319:     });
  320: 
  321:     const durationMs = Date.now() - startedAt;
  322: 
  323:     // Read --log-file content if adapter requested one (silent-fail diagnostic per codex F2)
  324:     let logFileContent = undefined;
  325:     if (logFilePath) {
  326:       try {
  327:         logFileContent = readFileSync(logFilePath, 'utf-8');
  328:       } catch (_) {
  329:         // log file may not exist if subprocess died early — that's diagnostic-worthy itself
  330:         logFileContent = undefined;
  331:       }
  332:     }
  333: 
  334:     return { exitCode, stdout, stderr, timedOut, timeoutReason, processCleanup, durationMs, logFileContent };
  335:   } finally {
  336:     releaseVendorLock();
  337:   }
  338: }
  339: 
  340: /**
  341:  * Kill an entire process tree (parent + descendants).
  342:  * Per codex v2.0.3 F3 + Phase 5 audit F1: NOT by port; tree-kill via OS-specific
  343:  * mechanism. Exported so hopper-runner can reuse it for background-mode timeout.
  344:  *
  345:  * @param {number} pid
  346:  * @param {boolean} isWindows
  347:  */
  348: export function killProcessTree(pid, isWindows) {
  349:   if (!pid) return { status: 'not-requested', method: null };
  350:   if (isWindows) {
  351:     // taskkill /T = kill tree (all child processes), /F = force
  352:     try {
  353:       execSync(`taskkill /PID ${pid} /T /F`, { stdio: 'ignore', timeout: 5000 });
  354:       return { status: 'succeeded', method: 'taskkill /T /F' };
  355:     } catch (_) {
  356:       return { status: 'failed', method: 'taskkill /T /F' };
  357:     }
  358:   } else {
  359:     // Unix: negative PID kills the process group (requires detached: true at spawn)
  360:     try {
  361:       process.kill(-pid, 'SIGKILL');
  362:       return { status: 'succeeded', method: 'process-group SIGKILL' };
  363:     } catch (groupError) {
  364:       // try direct kill as fallback
  365:       try {
  366:         process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL');
  367:         return { status: 'succeeded', method: 'direct SIGKILL' };
  368:       } catch (directError) {
  369:         // The process can exit naturally between timeout detection and cleanup.
  370:         // Preserve the existing success/failure status enum while making that
  371:         // benign race observable. Do not mask EPERM/EACCES or mixed failures.
  372:         if (groupError?.code === 'ESRCH' && directError?.code === 'ESRCH') {
  373:           return {
  374:             status: 'succeeded',
  375:             method: 'already exited (group/direct ESRCH)',
  376:             alreadyExited: true,
  377:           };
  378:         }
  379:         return { status: 'failed', method: 'direct SIGKILL' };
  380:       }
  381:     }
  382:   }
  383: }
  384: 
  385: /**
  386:  * HOPPER-6: best-effort check that a PID currently maps to an expected process
  387:  * image. Background jobs record the hopper-runner PID (a node process); on
  388:  * Windows especially, PIDs are recycled aggressively, so before `--stop` kills
  389:  * a tree we confirm the PID is still a node process and not some unrelated
  390:  * program that inherited the number. Returns 'match' | 'mismatch' | 'unknown'.
  391:  *
  392:  * Subprocess-based (tasklist/ps), so callers MUST NOT use it on the single-spawn
  393:  * dispatch path (spec §3 #4) — only in management commands like --stop, which
  394:  * already run outside the dispatch invariant. On any ambiguity it returns
  395:  * 'unknown' and leaves the kill/no-kill decision to the caller.
  396:  *
  397:  * HOPPER-6b (2026-08): on Linux, `ps -p <pid> -o comm=` reads the kernel's
  398:  * TASK_COMM field, capped at 15 visible characters. By default (no `--title`
  399:  * flag, which is the only call site that invokes uv_set_process_title outside
  400:  * user code) this should just be the plain basename of the exec'd binary
  401:  * ("node", 4 chars — well under the cap regardless of install path depth).
  402:  * But confirmed via real GitHub Actions job logs (not simulated, not
  403:  * reproduced by guesswork) that under Node 24 on ubuntu-latest, comm for a
  404:  * bog-standard `node --test` process comes back as something that does NOT
  405:  * contain "node" at all — while the exact same actions/setup-node install
  406:  * shape (`/opt/hostedtoolcache/node/<version>/x64/bin/node`) under Node 22 on
  407:  * the same runner image comes back matching. Path depth and OS were held
  408:  * constant; only the Node major version differed and only 24 broke, so this
  409:  * is a genuine Node-24-on-Linux behavior change, not a fluke of one run or a
  410:  * property of long install paths per se. We were not able to pin the exact
  411:  * upstream Node commit responsible (deps/uv is byte-identical at 1.51.0
  412:  * between the two lines, and the obvious call sites are unchanged), only that
  413:  * it reproduces deterministically in CI. Net effect: comm-only detection can
  414:  * falsely return 'mismatch' for a perfectly legitimate node process. That does
  415:  * not risk killing an unrelated process (background.js treats 'mismatch' as
  416:  * "never kill"), but it does silently break `--stop`'s ability to actually
  417:  * stop an in-progress job it legitimately owns — on Linux, under Node 24.
  418:  *
  419:  * Fix: on Linux, first resolve the executable via `/proc/<pid>/exe` (a
  420:  * kernel-maintained symlink to the exact binary that was exec'd, which cannot
  421:  * be spoofed by prctl/process-title tricks the way comm apparently can) and
  422:  * check ITS basename — this is exactly what comm was trying to approximate.
  423:  * `ps -o comm=` remains the fallback (permission-denied on /proc/<pid>/exe for
  424:  * a different-UID process, non-Linux POSIX, or /proc unavailable in some
  425:  * containers), so the original "flag a genuinely different process" guarantee
  426:  * is unchanged: this only ever turns a previously-false 'mismatch' for a real
  427:  * node PID into 'match'; it never turns a genuinely non-node PID into 'match'
  428:  * (deliberately not falling back to a full-command-line substring check,
  429:  * which could false-positive on a CLI argument that happens to contain
  430:  * "node").
  431:  *
  432:  * @param {number} pid
  433:  * @param {object} [opts]
  434:  * @param {string} [opts.expectImageIncludes]  case-insensitive substring (default 'node')
  435:  * @param {boolean} [opts.isWindows]
  436:  * @returns {'match'|'mismatch'|'unknown'}
  437:  */
  438: export function verifyPidImage(pid, { expectImageIncludes = 'node', isWindows = platform() === 'win32' } = {}) {
  439:   if (!pid || pid <= 0) return 'unknown';
  440:   const needle = String(expectImageIncludes).toLowerCase();
  441:   if (isWindows) {
  442:     try {
  443:       const out = execSync(`tasklist /FI "PID eq ${pid}" /FO CSV /NH`, {
  444:         encoding: 'utf-8', timeout: 5000, stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'],
  445:       });
  446:       if (!out || /no tasks/i.test(out)) return 'unknown';
  447:       // CSV row: "ImageName","PID","SessionName","Session#","MemUsage"
  448:       const image = (out.split(',')[0] || '').replace(/^"|"$/g, '').trim().toLowerCase();
  449:       if (!image) return 'unknown';
  450:       return image.includes(needle) ? 'match' : 'mismatch';
  451:     } catch (_) {
  452:       // tasklist failed, or PID not found → cannot determine
  453:       return 'unknown';
  454:     }
  455:   }
  456: 
  457:   let sawEvidence = false;
  458: 
  459:   // Linux only: untruncated executable path, sidesteps the 15-char comm
  460:   // truncation entirely (HOPPER-6b above).
  461:   if (platform() === 'linux') {
  462:     try {
  463:       const exe = readlinkSync(`/proc/${pid}/exe`).replace(/ \(deleted\)$/, '');
  464:       const base = (exe.split('/').pop() || '').toLowerCase();
  465:       if (base) {
  466:         sawEvidence = true;
  467:         if (base.includes(needle)) return 'match';
  468:       }
  469:     } catch (_) {
  470:       // ENOENT (pid gone/no such process), EACCES (different-user process —
  471:       // preserves the original "flag someone else's process" behavior via
  472:       // the ps fallback below), or /proc unavailable → fall through.
  473:     }
  474:   }
  475: 
  476:   try {
  477:     const out = execSync(`ps -p ${pid} -o comm=`, {
  478:       encoding: 'utf-8', timeout: 5000, stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'],
  479:     }).trim().toLowerCase();
  480:     if (out) {
  481:       sawEvidence = true;
  482:       if (out.includes(needle)) return 'match';
  483:     }
  484:   } catch (_) {
  485:     // ps failed, or PID not found → nothing more to learn from ps
  486:   }
  487: 
  488:   return sawEvidence ? 'mismatch' : 'unknown';
  489: }
  490: 
  491: /**
  492:  * Generate a unique log file path for a dispatch.
  493:  * Per codex v2.0.3 F2: unique per-dispatch to avoid stale-log false positives.
  494:  *
  495:  * @param {string} taskId
  496:  * @param {string} vendorName
  497:  * @returns {string} Absolute temp path
  498:  */
  499: export function makeUniqueLogPath(taskId, vendorName) {
  500:   const rand = Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 10);
  501:   const ts = Date.now();
  502:   return join(tmpdir(), `hopper-${vendorName}-${taskId}-${ts}-${rand}.log`);
  503: }
FILE cli/src/validation.js
    1: // Centralized task-id and flag validation (codex Phase 4 audit P1 fix)
    2: // Anchor: cli/src/validation.js
    3: //
    4: // Per Phase 4 audit: previously the regex `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,99}$`
    5: // was duplicated across:
    6: //   - commands/dispatch.md (prose in Claude Code slash command)
    7: //   - hosts/codex-cli/bin/hopper-codex (bash wrapper)
    8: //   - hosts/opencode/bin/hopper-opencode (bash wrapper)
    9: //   - cli/src/output.js (validateTaskId function)
   10: // and the dispatcher CLI itself had no equivalent validation at entry point.
   11: //
   12: // This module exports the canonical validation. The dispatcher CLI now calls
   13: // it before any work. Wrappers keep their inline regex (defense in depth +
   14: // fast-fail before subprocess invocation) but tests assert the patterns are
   15: // byte-equivalent.
   16: 
   17: import { isSafeModelIdentifier } from './public-identifiers.js';
   18: 
   19: /** Canonical task-id pattern. Matches what dispatch.md / hopper-codex / hopper-opencode enforce. */
   20: export const TASK_ID_PATTERN = /^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,99}$/;
   21: 
   22: /** Canonical bare flag whitelist for dispatch invocations. */
   23: export const ALLOWED_DISPATCH_FLAGS = Object.freeze(['--write', '--force', '--background', '--web-search']);
   24: 
   25: /** Value-taking flag whitelist (each consumes the next argv as its value). */
   26: export const ALLOWED_DISPATCH_VALUE_FLAGS = Object.freeze(['--model', '--reasoning', '--sandbox', '--timeout', '--vendor', '--subject-root']);
   27: 
   28: /** Vendor-name pattern (mirrors registered adapter naming: lowercase alnum/dash). */
   29: export const VENDOR_PATTERN = /^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,29}$/;
   30: 
   31: /**
   32:  * Validate a `--vendor` override name (pattern only; the dispatcher additionally
   33:  * checks it is a REGISTERED adapter and still enforces host != vendor). Throws on reject.
   34:  */
   35: export function validateVendor(name) {
   36:   if (typeof name !== 'string' || !VENDOR_PATTERN.test(name)) {
   37:     throw new Error(`--vendor "${name}" is not a valid vendor name (expected lowercase like codex / grok / kimi).`);
   38:   }
   39:   return name;
   40: }
   41: 
   42: /**
   43:  * Model-name pattern: alphanumeric + . - _ / : for namespaced model strings
   44:  * (`gpt-5.5`, `claude-opus-4-7`, `deepseek/v4-flash`, `org/model:tag`), PLUS
   45:  * space ( ) [ ] for the display-label aliases some vendors accept verbatim
   46:  * (claude `opus[1m]`/`sonnet[1m]`; agy `Gemini 3.5 Flash (High)`) — these are
   47:  * exactly the V4 normalizer's canonical outputs, so a user must be able to type
   48:  * them too. Injection-safe: every spawn passes the value as one argv element
   49:  * (no `shell: true`), and the leading [A-Za-z] still blocks `-flag` injection;
   50:  * shell metacharacters (; | & $ ` ' ") remain disallowed.
   51:  * Scope note: this relaxed regex applies to the JS dispatcher (CLI + dispatch.md),
   52:  * where every model value is passed as a discrete argv element (no shell). The Tier
   53:  * C bash wrappers under hosts/ (hopper-codex, hopper-cursor, etc.) deliberately keep
   54:  * the STRICTER pre-relaxation pattern, because they interpolate `--model $1` UNQUOTED
   55:  * into a prompt-embedded shell command — so spaces/parens must stay out there.
   56:  * Display-label aliases are therefore a JS-dispatcher feature, not a Tier C one.
   57:  */
   58: export const MODEL_PATTERN = /^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._/:()[\] -]{0,99}$/;
   59: 
   60: /**
   61:  * Reasoning effort whitelist. Matches codex CLI's vocabulary; OpenCode forwards
   62:  * an explicitly requested value as its provider-specific --variant, while Kimi
   63:  * and Claude have no prompt-mode effort argv. Other adapters may clamp or ignore
   64:  * the canonical values according to their own contracts.
   65:  *
   66:  * Per Phase 6b vendor-introspection research 2026-05-21: codex actually
   67:  * supports 5 levels (`minimal | low | medium | high | xhigh`), not 4.
   68:  * Our prior whitelist missed `minimal`. Source: official codex config-reference.
   69:  */
   70: export const ALLOWED_REASONING = Object.freeze(['minimal', 'low', 'medium', 'high', 'xhigh']);
   71: 
   72: /**
   73:  * Default reasoning/effort when a dispatch does not pass --reasoning. Product
   74:  * decision (owner 2026-06-16): max out by default; explicit --reasoning or
   75:  * HOPPER_DEFAULT_REASONING overrides. OpenCode deliberately does NOT forward this
   76:  * synthesized default: only a caller's explicit --reasoning becomes OpenCode's
   77:  * provider-specific --variant. Safe together with the idle-timeout primitive (a
   78:  * slow max-effort run is killed only for going SILENT, not for being slow).
   79:  */
   80: export const DEFAULT_DISPATCH_REASONING = 'xhigh';
   81: 
   82: /**
   83:  * Resolve the effective default reasoning: HOPPER_DEFAULT_REASONING (if a legal
   84:  * level) > DEFAULT_DISPATCH_REASONING. Never throws (invalid env is ignored).
   85:  * @returns {string}
   86:  */
   87: export function resolveDefaultReasoning() {
   88:   const env = process.env.HOPPER_DEFAULT_REASONING;
   89:   if (env && ALLOWED_REASONING.includes(env)) return env;
   90:   return DEFAULT_DISPATCH_REASONING;
   91: }
   92: 
   93: /**
   94:  * Canonical sandbox / permission vocabulary for dispatch.
   95:  *
   96:  * `danger-full-access` is the product default for implementation work. The
   97:  * dispatcher only downgrades to `read-only` when the task text explicitly says
   98:  * it is read-only, or when the user passes --sandbox read-only.
   99:  */
  100: export const ALLOWED_SANDBOXES = Object.freeze(['read-only', 'workspace-write', 'danger-full-access']);
  101: export const DEFAULT_DISPATCH_SANDBOX = 'danger-full-access';
  102: 
  103: /**
  104:  * Resolve the effective DEFAULT sandbox when a dispatch neither passes --sandbox
  105:  * nor matches a more-specific rule (read-only task text or a read-only task-type).
  106:  * HOPPER_DEFAULT_SANDBOX (if a legal mode) > DEFAULT_DISPATCH_SANDBOX. Lets a
  107:  * safety-conscious operator flip the global baseline (e.g. to workspace-write or
  108:  * read-only) without editing each dispatch. Never throws (invalid env ignored).
  109:  * @returns {string}
  110:  */
  111: export function resolveDefaultSandbox() {
  112:   const env = process.env.HOPPER_DEFAULT_SANDBOX;
  113:   if (env && ALLOWED_SANDBOXES.includes(env)) return env;
  114:   return DEFAULT_DISPATCH_SANDBOX;
  115: }
  116: 
  117: /**
  118:  * Task-types that default to a READ-ONLY vendor sandbox — review / research work
  119:  * that must not edit the repo. This is the opt-in policy layer that DOES infer
  120:  * read-only from task-type, sitting BELOW explicit --sandbox and read-only task
  121:  * text in precedence. A task that genuinely needs to write still wins by passing
  122:  * --sandbox workspace-write / danger-full-access.
  123:  */
  124: export const READ_ONLY_DEFAULT_TASK_TYPES = Object.freeze([
  125:   'code-review-adversarial',
  126:   'code-review-acceptance',
  127:   'decision-review',
  128:   'spec-blindspot-hunt',
  129:   'prd-research',
  130:   'tech-research',
  131:   'market-research',
  132: ]);
  133: 
  134: /**
  135:  * Task-types that AUTO-ENABLE web search (defined as web-needing). The dispatch
  136:  * layer sets opts.webSearch=true for these unless an explicit --web-search has
  137:  * already set it; only web-capable adapters act on it (codex/claude/copilot).
  138:  */
  139: export const WEB_SEARCH_TASK_TYPES = Object.freeze([
  140:   'prd-research',
  141:   // `tech-research` evaluates implementation approaches, which almost always means
  142:   // reading current upstream docs, release notes and issue trackers — the same
  143:   // web-needing shape as product research, so it auto-enables the same way.
  144:   // `decision-review` deliberately does NOT: adjudicating a fork the host has
  145:   // already framed is a judgment over supplied context, not a survey.
  146:   'tech-research',
  147:   'market-research',
  148: ]);
  149: 
  150: /**
  151:  * Legal queue status values per .hopper/queue.md schema convention.
  152:  * Per codex final strict audit P1 (Category A): the parser previously mapped
  153:  * unknown statuses to 'pending' silently, which meant a "failure-detected"
  154:  * task would be re-eligibilized. Statuses are now validated explicitly.
  155:  */
  156: export const LEGAL_QUEUE_STATUSES = Object.freeze([
  157:   'pending',
  158:   'in-progress',
  159:   'done',
  160:   'failed',
  161:   'removed',
  162: ]);
  163: 
  164: /**
  165:  * Task-type pattern. Names a .hopper/tasks/<type>.md file directly, so it
  166:  * needs the same path-safety guarantees as task-id.
  167:  * Per codex final strict audit P1 (Category E): tasks.js previously did
  168:  * `join(hopperDir, 'tasks', `${taskType}.md`)` without validation; a
  169:  * malicious queue row could escape via '../' or absolute path.
  170:  */
  171: export const TASK_TYPE_PATTERN = /^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,49}$/;
  172: 
  173: /**
  174:  * Validate a task ID. Throws on rejection.
  175:  * Per codex Phase 3 F3 + Phase 4 P1: regex + explicit '..' rejection.
  176:  */
  177: export function validateTaskId(id) {
  178:   if (typeof id !== 'string') throw new Error(`task-id must be string, got ${typeof id}`);
  179:   if (id.length === 0) throw new Error('task-id must not be empty');
  180:   if (id.length > 100) throw new Error(`task-id exceeds 100 chars (got ${id.length})`);
  181:   if (!TASK_ID_PATTERN.test(id)) {
  182:     throw new Error(`task-id "${id}" contains unsafe characters. ` +
  183:       `Allowed: ^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,99}$ (no slashes, no leading dot).`);
  184:   }
  185:   if (id.includes('..')) {
  186:     throw new Error(`task-id "${id}" contains '..' (path traversal).`);
  187:   }
  188: }
  189: 
  190: /**
  191:  * Validate that all bare flags are in the allowed whitelist. Throws on rejection.
  192:  * @param {string[]} flags
  193:  */
  194: export function validateDispatchFlags(flags) {
  195:   for (const f of flags) {
  196:     if (!ALLOWED_DISPATCH_FLAGS.includes(f)) {
  197:       throw new Error(`Invalid flag "${f}". Allowed: ${ALLOWED_DISPATCH_FLAGS.join(', ')}.`);
  198:     }
  199:   }
  200: }
  201: 
  202: /**
  203:  * Validate a model name. Throws on rejection.
  204:  *
  205:  * Note: model strings are passed as argv to vendor CLIs, not used as file
  206:  * paths, so the regex enforces shell-safety (no metachars, no spaces) rather
  207:  * than path-safety. The character class intentionally permits `/` and `:`
  208:  * because real model names like `deepseek/v4-flash` and `org/model:tag` use
  209:  * them; these are not interpreted as path separators by the receiving CLI.
  210:  */
  211: export function validateModelName(model) {
  212:   if (typeof model !== 'string') throw new Error(`--model value must be string, got ${typeof model}`);
  213:   if (model.length === 0) throw new Error('--model value must not be empty');
  214:   if (!MODEL_PATTERN.test(model) || !isSafeModelIdentifier(model)) {
  215:     throw new Error('--model contains unsafe characters or an unsafe model identifier. ' +
  216:       `Allowed: ${MODEL_PATTERN.source} — letters/digits . _ / : plus space ( ) [ ] for ` +
  217:       `display-label aliases; no shell metachars (; | & $ \` ' " < >) and no leading '-'.`);
  218:   }
  219: }
  220: 
  221: /**
  222:  * Validate a reasoning effort level. Throws on rejection.
  223:  */
  224: export function validateReasoning(reasoning) {
  225:   if (typeof reasoning !== 'string') throw new Error(`--reasoning value must be string, got ${typeof reasoning}`);
  226:   if (!ALLOWED_REASONING.includes(reasoning)) {
  227:     throw new Error(`--reasoning "${reasoning}" invalid. Allowed: ${ALLOWED_REASONING.join(', ')}.`);
  228:   }
  229: }
  230: 
  231: /**
  232:  * Validate a per-dispatch --timeout value (the absolute ceiling, in ms). Throws
  233:  * on rejection. Range-guarded so a typo can neither be 0/negative nor absurdly
  234:  * large. Returns the parsed integer.
  235:  * @param {string|number} value
  236:  * @returns {number}
  237:  */
  238: export const MIN_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 1_000;
  239: export const MAX_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 21_600_000;  // 6h hard sanity ceiling
  240: export function validateTimeout(value) {
  241:   // codex review P2: reject partial/float inputs ("600000abc", "1000.5") rather
  242:   // than letting Number.parseInt truncate them. Require a whole, all-digits value.
  243:   let ms;
  244:   if (typeof value === 'number') {
  245:     if (!Number.isInteger(value)) throw new Error(`--timeout ${value} is not an integer (milliseconds).`);
  246:     ms = value;
  247:   } else {
  248:     const s = String(value).trim();
  249:     if (!/^\d+$/.test(s)) throw new Error(`--timeout "${value}" is not an integer (milliseconds).`);
  250:     ms = Number.parseInt(s, 10);
  251:   }
  252:   if (ms < MIN_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS) throw new Error(`--timeout ${ms} too small (min ${MIN_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS}ms).`);
  253:   if (ms > MAX_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS) throw new Error(`--timeout ${ms} too large (max ${MAX_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS}ms = 6h).`);
  254:   return ms;
  255: }
  256: 
  257: /**
  258:  * Validate a dispatch sandbox / permission mode. Throws on rejection.
  259:  */
  260: export function validateSandbox(sandbox) {
  261:   if (typeof sandbox !== 'string') throw new Error(`--sandbox value must be string, got ${typeof sandbox}`);
  262:   if (!ALLOWED_SANDBOXES.includes(sandbox)) {
  263:     throw new Error(`--sandbox "${sandbox}" invalid. Allowed: ${ALLOWED_SANDBOXES.join(', ')}.`);
  264:   }
  265: }
  266: 
  267: /**
  268:  * Vendor/host FAMILY map for the host!=vendor isomorphism guard. Two identities
  269:  * in the SAME family are the same underlying product/company self-dispatching
  270:  * through a different invocation surface — e.g. a Claude Code session (host
  271:  * identity 'claude-code', see cli/src/host-detect.js) calling out to the
  272:  * `claude` vendor adapter (`claude -p`) is literally Anthropic's own Claude
  273:  * Code CLI talking to itself, just via a different entry point.
  274:  *
  275:  * Comparison is by family, NOT raw string equality, because the host identity
  276:  * and the vendor name are not always the same string (host 'claude-code' vs
  277:  * vendor 'claude' — there is no Tier-C wrapper that could ever set
  278:  * HOPPER_HOST_VENDOR=claude-code, so a plain `===` check would never catch
  279:  * this pair). For the 5 existing Tier-C wrappers, HOPPER_HOST_VENDOR already
  280:  * equals the vendor's own name (codex, grok, copilot, opencode, cursor), so
  281:  * this map's entries double as both the "host" and "vendor" side lookup key
  282:  * for those.
  283:  *
  284:  * Basis for each mapped entry (checked against cli/src/vendors/*.js headers +
  285:  * behavior 2026-07-31, not guessed):
  286:  * - claude / claude-code -> anthropic: cli/src/vendors/claude.js — "Anthropic's
  287:  *   first-party agentic coding CLI 'Claude Code'". 'claude-code' is the host
  288:  *   identity produced by host-detect.js's self-detection (no wrapper sets it).
  289:  * - codex -> openai: cli/src/vendors/codex.js — wraps `codex exec`, OpenAI's
  290:  *   own Codex CLI; hosts/codex-cli's wrapper dispatches through the SAME CLI.
  291:  * - grok -> xai: cli/src/vendors/grok.js header — "xAI's official first-party
  292:  *   agentic coding CLI 'Grok Build'".
  293:  * - kimi -> moonshot: cli/src/vendors/kimi.js header (Kimi Code CLI, Moonshot
  294:  *   AI's own product); hosts/claude-code's auth table lists MOONSHOT_API_KEY.
  295:  *
  296:  * Deliberately NOT mapped (do not guess a family for these — checked and found
  297:  * genuinely multi-backend, per 2026-07-31 audit):
  298:  * - copilot: cli/src/vendors/copilot.js capabilities.modelArg.knownGood spans
  299:  *   claude-family, gpt-family, AND gemini-family model names depending on the
  300:  *   user's GitHub Copilot subscription tier — Copilot is a multi-backend
  301:  *   router, not one model company. An unmapped name never matches any family,
  302:  *   so copilot-cli host +
  303:  *   copilot vendor (the one case that IS the same product self-dispatching)
  304:  *   is NOT currently blocked by this guard — a known gap, out of scope here
  305:  *   (only `claude` was in scope for this pass).
  306:  * - opencode / mimo: both adapters document themselves as provider-agnostic
  307:  *   routers — opencode.js: "actual catalog depends on the user's opencode auth
  308:  *   configuration... NOT on this adapter"; mimo.js: "MiMoCode fork" accepting
  309:  *   arbitrary `--model <provider/model>` pairs. No single family applies.
  310:  * - agy: cli/src/vendors/agy.js capabilities.modelArg.knownGood is all
  311:  *   Gemini-labeled (suggesting Google), but the same comment notes Claude/
  312:  *   GPT-OSS labels also appear in the picker (plan-gated) — i.e. agy is ALSO
  313:  *   multi-backend, just Gemini-default. Left unmapped rather than guessed.
  314:  * - cursor: hosts/cursor-cli sets HOPPER_HOST_VENDOR=cursor, but there is no
  315:  *   registered `cursor` vendor adapter at all (cli/src/vendors/index.js) —
  316:  *   nothing to map on the vendor side, so this host can never self-match.
  317:  */
  318: export const VENDOR_FAMILY = Object.freeze({
  319:   claude: 'anthropic',
  320:   'claude-code': 'anthropic',
  321:   codex: 'openai',
  322:   grok: 'xai',
  323:   kimi: 'moonshot',
  324: });
  325: 
  326: /**
  327:  * Enforce the product rule that a host must not dispatch back into the same
  328:  * vendor identity/family.
  329:  *
  330:  * hostVendor may be omitted entirely (`undefined`/empty) by a caller that has
  331:  * no signal at all — that legacy standalone path stays fully silent (returns
  332:  * `{ enforced: false }`) for backward compatibility. But once a caller HAS a
  333:  * hostVendor value (whether from the explicit HOPPER_HOST_VENDOR env, set by
  334:  * every Tier-C wrapper, or from host-detect.js's self-detection, which always
  335:  * returns a concrete string — see HOST_UNKNOWN there), this function never
  336:  * silently no-ops again: if that value's family cannot be resolved (including
  337:  * the literal 'unknown' self-detection sentinel, or any future/unrecognized
  338:  * host string), it returns `{ enforced: false, notice }` where `notice`
  339:  * explicitly says the isomorphism check did not run — callers MUST surface
  340:  * this, not swallow it (this project treats silent skips as a defect class).
  341:  *
  342:  * @param {string | undefined} hostVendor
  343:  * @param {string} resolvedVendor
  344:  * @returns {{ enforced: boolean, notice?: string }}
  345:  */
  346: export function validateHostVendorSeparation(hostVendor, resolvedVendor) {
  347:   if (!hostVendor) return { enforced: false };
  348:   if (typeof resolvedVendor !== 'string' || resolvedVendor.length === 0) {
  349:     throw new Error(`resolved vendor must be non-empty string, got ${typeof resolvedVendor}`);
  350:   }
  351:   const hostFamily = VENDOR_FAMILY[hostVendor];
  352:   if (!hostFamily) {
  353:     return {
  354:       enforced: false,
  355:       notice: `host '${hostVendor}' not recognized (no known vendor-family mapping) — ` +
  356:         `host != vendor isomorphism check NOT run for this dispatch.`,
  357:     };
  358:   }
  359:   const vendorFamily = VENDOR_FAMILY[resolvedVendor];
  360:   if (vendorFamily && hostFamily === vendorFamily) {
  361:     throw new Error(
  362:       `Host '${hostVendor}' cannot dispatch to the same vendor '${resolvedVendor}' (both are ${hostFamily}: host != vendor). ` +
  363:       `hopper-plugin requires host != vendor. Choose a different vendor in .hopper/AGENTS.md or invoke from a different host.`
  364:     );
  365:   }
  366:   return { enforced: true };
  367: }
  368: 
  369: /**
  370:  * Validate a task-type string. Per codex final strict audit P1 (Category E):
  371:  * task-type names a `.hopper/tasks/<type>.md` file path, so it must be
  372:  * lowercase, kebab-case, no path separators, no '..'.
  373:  */
  374: export function validateTaskType(taskType) {
  375:   if (typeof taskType !== 'string') throw new Error(`task-type must be string, got ${typeof taskType}`);
  376:   if (taskType.length === 0) throw new Error('task-type must not be empty');
  377:   if (!TASK_TYPE_PATTERN.test(taskType)) {
  378:     throw new Error(`task-type "${taskType}" contains unsafe characters. ` +
  379:       `Allowed: ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,49}$ (lowercase kebab-case, no slashes, no '..').`);
  380:   }
  381:   if (taskType.includes('..')) {
  382:     throw new Error(`task-type "${taskType}" contains '..' (path traversal).`);
  383:   }
  384: }
  385: 
  386: /**
  387:  * Returns true if status is a legal queue status, false otherwise.
  388:  * Caller decides whether to throw or just warn.
  389:  */
  390: export function isLegalQueueStatus(status) {
  391:   return LEGAL_QUEUE_STATUSES.includes(status);
  392: }

  190:  * @returns {Promise<import('./types.js').SubprocessResult>}

  192: export async function runSubprocessOnce({
  193:   command,
  194:   args,
  195:   stdinInput,
  196:   timeoutMs,
  197:   idleMs = 0,
  198:   env,
  199:   cwd,
  200:   logFilePath = null,
  201:   vendorName,
  202:   subjectRoot = null,
  203:   sandbox,
  204:   subjectGuardOptions,
  205: }) {
  206:   const isWindows = platform() === 'win32';
  207:   const startedAt = Date.now();
  208:   // Validation/backend availability is deliberately before the vendor spawn and
  209:   // before lock acquisition: a requested guard is mandatory, never advisory.
  210:   const subjectGuard = prepareSubjectRootGuard({ subjectRoot, sandbox, ...(subjectGuardOptions || {}) });
  211:   const invocation = wrapSubjectRootInvocation(command, args, subjectGuard);
  212:   const releaseVendorLock = await acquireVendorLock(vendorName);
  213: 
  214:   try {
  215:     const child = spawn(invocation.command, invocation.args, {
  216:       env: { ...process.env, ...(env || {}) },
  217:       cwd: cwd || process.cwd(),
  218:       stdio: [stdinInput == null ? 'ignore' : 'pipe', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  219:       detached: !isWindows,                                       // Unix: detached process group for tree-kill
  220:       windowsHide: isWindows,
  221:     });
  222: 
  223:     let stdout = '';
  224:     let stderr = '';
  225:     let timedOut = false;
  226:     let timeoutReason = null;     // 'idle' | 'ceiling' (diagnostic)
  227:     let processCleanup = { status: 'not-needed', method: null };
  228:     let killTimer = null;
  229:     let idleTimer = null;
  230:     let windowsCleanupTimer = null;
  231:     let settleExit = null;
  232:     let exitSettled = false;
  233: 
  234:     const clearTimeoutTimers = () => {
  235:       if (killTimer) clearTimeout(killTimer);
  236:       if (idleTimer) clearTimeout(idleTimer);
  237:       if (windowsCleanupTimer) clearTimeout(windowsCleanupTimer);
  238:       killTimer = null;
  239:       idleTimer = null;
  240:       windowsCleanupTimer = null;
  280:     // idle timer — resets on every chunk of vendor output; fires only after
  281:     // idleMs of total silence (hung / reverse-pressured). ADDITIVE: when idleMs
  282:     // is falsy this is a no-op, so callers/tests passing only timeoutMs keep the
  283:     // legacy single-ceiling behavior unchanged.
  284:     const armIdle = () => {
  285:       if (!(idleMs > 0) || timedOut) return;
  286:       if (idleTimer) clearTimeout(idleTimer);
  287:       idleTimer = setTimeout(() => claimTimeout('idle'), idleMs);
  288:       if (idleTimer.unref) idleTimer.unref();
  289:     };
  290: 
  291:     child.stdout.on('data', (chunk) => { stdout += chunk.toString(); armIdle(); });
  292:     child.stderr.on('data', (chunk) => { stderr += chunk.toString(); armIdle(); });
  293: 
  294:     if (stdinInput != null && child.stdin) {
  295:       try {
  296:         child.stdin.write(stdinInput);
  297:         child.stdin.end();
  298:       } catch (_) {
  299:         // child may have exited before stdin ready — ignore; exit code captures result
  300:       }
  301:     }
  302: 
  303:     // Absolute ceiling. NO retry on timeout — timeout = failure, surface it.
  304:     if (timeoutMs > 0) {
  305:       killTimer = setTimeout(() => claimTimeout('ceiling'), timeoutMs);
  306:     }
  307:     armIdle();  // start the idle clock at spawn (silence before first byte counts)
  308: 
  309:     const exitCode = await new Promise((resolve) => {
  310:       settleExit = (code, signal) => {
  311:         if (exitSettled) return;
  312:         exitSettled = true;
  313:         clearTimeoutTimers();
  314:         // Map signal to exit-code-equivalent (POSIX convention: 128 + signal)
  315:         resolve(code != null ? code : 128 + (signal === 'SIGKILL' ? 9 : signal === 'SIGTERM' ? 15 : 0));
  316:       };
  317:       child.on('close', settleExit);
  318:       child.on('error', (_err) => settleExit(127, null)); // command-not-found convention
  319:     });
  320: 
  321:     const durationMs = Date.now() - startedAt;
  322: 
  323:     // Read --log-file content if adapter requested one (silent-fail diagnostic per codex F2)
  324:     let logFileContent = undefined;
  325:     if (logFilePath) {
  326:       try {
  327:         logFileContent = readFileSync(logFilePath, 'utf-8');
  328:       } catch (_) {
  329:         // log file may not exist if subprocess died early — that's diagnostic-worthy itself
  330:         logFileContent = undefined;
  331:       }
  332:     }
  333: 
  334:     return { exitCode, stdout, stderr, timedOut, timeoutReason, processCleanup, durationMs, logFileContent };
  335:   } finally {
  336:     releaseVendorLock();
  337:   }

  186:   parseResult(raw) {
  187:     if (raw.timedOut) {
  188:       return adapterFailure('timeout', 'adapter-timeout');
  189:     }
  190:     if (raw.exitCode === 127 || /not found|command not found/i.test(raw.stderr || '')) {
  191:       return adapterFailure('permission-fail', 'adapter-binary-missing');
  192:     }
  193:     // A completed result object is the authoritative vendor outcome. The
  194:     // file-backed runner records startup diagnostics in the same log, so broad
  195:     // auth-shaped text (for example an env-precedence notice) must not override
  196:     // a clean `result/success/is_error:false` envelope.
  197:     const parsedResult = extractClaudeResult(raw.stdout);
  198:     if (raw.exitCode === 0 && parsedResult && !parsedResult.isError && parsedResult.text.trim()) {

  200:     }

  203:     // authentication_failed / oauth_org_not_allowed — code.claude.com/docs/en/headless).
  204:     if (/authentication_failed|oauth_org_not_allowed|invalid api key|invalid x-api-key|ANTHROPIC_API_KEY|not logged in|please run\b.*login|\b401\b|\b403\b/i.test(signal)) {
  205:       return failedClaudeOutput('auth-fail', 'adapter-auth-failed', parsedResult);
  206:     }
  207:     // Billing / credit / usage-limit block. Kept as a distinct branch because the
  208:     // SDK emits a `billing_error` category regardless of the current billing
  209:     // model — the wording stays policy-neutral on purpose (the `claude -p` billing
  210:     // regime changed repeatedly across 2026; see the BILLING NOTE at the top).
  211:     if (/billing_error|credit balance|insufficient.*credit|agent sdk credit|quota exceeded|usage limit/i.test(signal)) {
  212:       return failedClaudeOutput('auth-fail', 'adapter-auth-failed', parsedResult);
  213:     }
  214:     if (raw.exitCode === 0) {
  215:       // --output-format json yields a single trailing result object. Parse
  216:       // defensively (whole-stdout JSON → last JSON line for stream-json safety →
  217:       // raw text for plain --output-format text).
  218:       // FAIL FAST instead of recording a silent empty result: a blocked headless
  219:       // turn (permission mode) or a max-turns hit can exit 0 with is_error/empty
  220:       // result. Treat is_error OR no usable text as failure.
  221:       if (parsedResult.isError || !parsedResult.text.trim()) {
  222:         return failedClaudeOutput('unknown-fail', 'adapter-protocol-invalid', parsedResult);
  223:       }

  229: 
  230: /**
  231:  * Defensive extraction of the answer text from `claude -p --output-format json`
  232:  * stdout. The success object shape (code.claude.com/docs/en/headless) is:
  233:  *   { type:"result", subtype:"success"|"error_max_turns"|"error_during_execution",
  234:  *     is_error:boolean, result:"<text>", session_id, total_cost_usd, usage:{...} }
  235:  * Falls back to the last JSON line (stream-json) then to raw text (plain
  236:  * --output-format text, or any unparseable payload) so a format drift degrades
  237:  * gracefully instead of throwing.
  238:  * @param {string} stdout
  239:  * @returns {{ text: string, usage?: object, isError: boolean, subtype?: string, terminalEnvelope?: object|null, answerSource: 'result'|'text-fallback'|'raw-fallback'|'none' }}
  240:  */
  241: function extractClaudeResult(stdout) {
  242:   const trimmed = (stdout || '').trim();
  243:   if (!trimmed) return { text: '', isError: false, answerSource: 'none' };
  244:   const noAnswerFromEnvelope = (obj) => ({
  245:     text: '',
  246:     isError: obj.is_error === true || /^error/i.test(String(obj.subtype || '')),
  247:     subtype: obj.subtype,
  248:     terminalEnvelope: obj,
  249:     answerSource: 'none',
  250:   });
  251:   const fromObj = (obj) => {
  252:     if (!obj || typeof obj !== 'object' || Array.isArray(obj)) return null;
  253:     const answerSource = typeof obj.result === 'string' ? 'result'
  254:       : (typeof obj.text === 'string' ? 'text-fallback' : null);
  255:     const text = answerSource === 'result' ? obj.result
  256:       : (answerSource === 'text-fallback' ? obj.text : null);
  257:     if (text === null) return null;
  258:     const isError = obj.is_error === true || /^error/i.test(String(obj.subtype || ''));
  259:     let usage = (obj.usage && typeof obj.usage === 'object') ? { ...obj.usage } : undefined;
  260:     if (typeof obj.total_cost_usd === 'number') usage = { ...(usage || {}), totalCostUsd: obj.total_cost_usd };
  261:     return { text, usage, isError, subtype: obj.subtype, terminalEnvelope: obj, answerSource };
  262:   };
  263:   try {
  264:     const envelope = JSON.parse(trimmed);
  265:     const got = fromObj(envelope);
  266:     if (got) return got;
  267:     if (envelope && typeof envelope === 'object' && !Array.isArray(envelope)) return noAnswerFromEnvelope(envelope);
  268:   } catch (_) { /* not a single JSON object — scan lines for stream-json safety */ }
  269:   const lines = trimmed.split(/\r?\n/).filter((l) => l.trim());
  270:   let lastEnvelopeWithoutText = null;
  271:   for (let i = lines.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
  272:     try {
  273:       const envelope = JSON.parse(lines[i]);
  274:       const got = fromObj(envelope);
  275:       if (got) return got;
  276:       if (!lastEnvelopeWithoutText && envelope && typeof envelope === 'object' && !Array.isArray(envelope)) {
  277:         lastEnvelopeWithoutText = noAnswerFromEnvelope(envelope);
  278:       }
  279:     } catch (_) { /* keep scanning upward */ }
  280:   }
  281:   if (lastEnvelopeWithoutText) return lastEnvelopeWithoutText;
  282:   // Plain text (default --output-format text) or unparseable → use stdout as-is.
  283:   return { text: trimmed, isError: false, terminalEnvelope: null, answerSource: 'raw-fallback' };
  284: }
  285: 
  286: function successfulClaudeOutput(parsed) {
  287:   const output = parsed.usage
  288:     ? { text: parsed.text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none', usage: parsed.usage }
  289:     : { text: parsed.text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none' };
  290:   if (isVerifiedClaudeResult(parsed)) {
  291:     output.outputEvidence = {
  292:       completeness: 'verified-complete',
  293:       source: 'structured-envelope',
  294:       terminalMarker: 'claude-result-success',
  295:     };
  296:   }
  297:   const modelAttestation = extractClaudeModelAttestation(parsed.terminalEnvelope);
  298:   return modelAttestation ? { ...output, modelAttestation } : output;
  299: }
  300: 
  301: function failedClaudeOutput(status, diagnosticCode, parsed) {
  302:   const failure = adapterFailure(status, diagnosticCode);
  303:   if (!parsed || parsed.answerSource !== 'result' || !parsed.text.trim()) return failure;
  304:   return {
  305:     ...failure,
  306:     text: parsed.text,
  307:     outputEvidence: {
  308:       completeness: 'unknown-completeness',
  309:       source: 'structured-envelope',
  310:       terminalMarker: 'none',
  311:     },
  312:   };
  313: }
  314: 
  315: function isVerifiedClaudeResult(parsed) {
  316:   const envelope = parsed?.terminalEnvelope;
  317:   return parsed?.answerSource === 'result'
  318:     && envelope?.type === 'result'
  319:     && envelope?.subtype === 'success'
  320:     && envelope?.is_error === false;
  321: }

  132:     if (/invalid authentication|api key.*(invalid|expired)|verify your membership|usage limit|unauthoriz|not logged in|\b401\b|\b40[23]\b/i.test(signal)) {
  133:       return adapterFailure('auth-fail', 'adapter-auth-failed');

  138:       return adapterFailure('auth-fail', 'adapter-auth-failed');

  141:       // 0.x sends the "To resume this session: kimi -r <id>" hint to STDERR, so this
  142:       // strip is now a defensive no-op for the new tool (still strips the legacy 1.x

  144:       // no approved terminal actual-model field yet, so this stays config-only and
  145:       // deliberately never attaches modelAttestation.

  147:       return { text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none' };
  148:     }
  149:     return adapterFailure('unknown-fail', 'adapter-unknown-failed');

  135:   parseResult(raw) {
  136:     const parsed = parseOpencodeAnswerEvents(raw.stdout);
  137:     const outputEvidence = parsed.text
  138:       ? (parsed.terminalMarker === 'none'
  139:         ? { completeness: 'unknown-completeness', source: parsed.recoveredFromPlainText ? 'ansi-stripped-plain-text' : 'event-stream', terminalMarker: 'none' }
  140:         : { completeness: 'verified-complete', source: 'event-stream', terminalMarker: parsed.terminalMarker })
  141:       : undefined;
  142:     if (raw.timedOut) {
  143:       return { ...adapterFailure('timeout', 'adapter-timeout'), text: parsed.text, outputEvidence };
  144:     }
  145:     if (raw.exitCode === 127) {
  146:       return adapterFailure('permission-fail', 'adapter-binary-missing');
  147:     }
  148:     if (raw.exitCode === 0 && parsed.text && parsed.terminalMarker !== 'none' && !parsed.recoveredFromPlainText) {
  149:       const modelAttestation = extractOpencodeModelAttestation(raw.stdout);
  150:       return modelAttestation
  151:         ? { text: parsed.text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none', outputEvidence, modelAttestation }
  152:         : { text: parsed.text, status: 'success', diagnosticCode: 'none', outputEvidence };
  153:     }
  154:     return {
  155:       ...adapterFailure('unknown-fail', raw.exitCode === 0 ? 'adapter-protocol-invalid' : 'adapter-unknown-failed'),
  156:       text: parsed.text,
  157:       outputEvidence,
  158:     };
  159:   },

  161: 
  162: /**
  163:  * Strip ANSI escape sequences (CSI color/cursor codes emitted by opencode's
  164:  * pretty log mode) and stray carriage returns so a line can be JSON.parsed or
  165:  * read as plain text. ISSUE-opencode-ansi-log-output-not-parsed: a captured
  166:  * background log on Windows contained ANSI-wrapped lines instead of clean
  167:  * NDJSON.
  168:  * @param {string} line
  169:  * @returns {string}
  170:  */
  171: function stripOpencodeAnsi(line) {
  172:   return String(line)
  173:     // CSI sequences: ESC [ params intermediates final-byte
  174:     .replace(/\x1B\[[0-9;?]*[ -/]*[@-~]/g, '')
  175:     // OSC sequences: ESC ] … (BEL|ESC\) terminated
  176:     .replace(/\x1B\][^\x07\x1B]*(?:\x07|\x1B\\)/g, '')
  177:     .replace(/\r/g, '');
  178: }
  179: 
  180: function parseOpencodeAnswerEvents(stdout) {
  181:   const trimmed = (stdout || '').trim();
  182:   if (!trimmed) return { text: '', terminalMarker: 'none', recoveredFromPlainText: false };
  183: 
  184:   const rawLines = trimmed.split(/\r?\n/);
  185:   // Only an input that actually LOOKS like an opencode log (ANSI escapes,
  186:   // `> build` banner, `→` tool traces, <thinking> blocks) qualifies for
  187:   // plain-text recovery — arbitrary raw stdout must stay fail-closed and
  188:   // never becomes parser evidence (vendors-contract privacy test).
  189:   const looksLikeOpencodeLog = rawLines.some((line) =>
  190:     /\x1B\[/.test(line) || /^\s*>\s*\S/.test(stripOpencodeAnsi(line)) ||
  191:     /^[→▶]/.test(stripOpencodeAnsi(line).trim()) || line.includes('<thinking>'));
  192:   const lines = rawLines.map(stripOpencodeAnsi).filter((line) => line.trim());
  193:   const chunks = [];
  194:   let terminalMarker = 'none';
  195: 
  196:   for (const line of lines) {
  197:     try {
  198:       const event = JSON.parse(line);
  199:       visitOpencodeProtocolEvents(event, (candidate) => {
  200:         const text = extractOpencodeEventText(candidate);
  201:         if (text) chunks.push(text);
  202:         const marker = opencodeTerminalMarker(candidate);
  203:         if (marker !== 'none') terminalMarker = marker;
  204:       });
  205:     } catch (_) {
  206:       // Mixed stdout is possible; never treat unstructured lines as answers.
  207:     }
  208:   }
  209: 
  210:   const text = chunks.join('').trim();
  211:   if (text || terminalMarker !== 'none') {
  212:     return { text, terminalMarker, recoveredFromPlainText: false };
  213:   }
  214: 
  215:   // Conservative plain-text recovery (ISSUE-opencode-ansi-log-output-not-
  216:   // parsed): when ZERO JSON events parse — the observed failure mode was an
  217:   // ANSI pretty log instead of an NDJSON stream — keep the readable residue
  218:   // as recovered text so the run record shows what the vendor actually said.
  219:   // This NEVER flips the classification to success: terminalMarker stays
  220:   // 'none' and recoveredFromPlainText marks the text as unverified.
  221:   const recovered = looksLikeOpencodeLog ? recoverOpencodePlainText(lines) : '';
  222:   return { text: recovered, terminalMarker: 'none', recoveredFromPlainText: Boolean(recovered) };
  223: }
  224: 
  225: /**
  226:  * Best-effort readable text from a non-JSON (ANSI-stripped) log: drop runner
  227:  * notices, structured INFO logs, the `> build` banner, `→` tool-trace lines,
  228:  * and <thinking> blocks; keep the remaining substantive lines.
  229:  * @param {string[]} lines ANSI-stripped, non-empty lines
  230:  * @returns {string}
  231:  */
  232: function recoverOpencodePlainText(lines) {
  233:   const kept = [];
  234:   let inThinking = false;
  235:   for (const line of lines) {
  236:     const l = line.trim();
  237:     if (!l) continue;
  238:     if (l.includes('<thinking>') && l.includes('</thinking>')) continue;      // single-line thinking block
  239:     if (l.includes('<thinking>')) { inThinking = true; continue; }
  240:     if (l.includes('</thinking>')) { inThinking = false; continue; }
  241:     if (inThinking) continue;
  242:     if (/^hopper-runner:/.test(l)) continue;                                  // runner notices
  243:     if (/^timestamp=.*\blevel=(INFO|WARN|ERROR|DEBUG)\b/.test(l)) continue;   // opencode structured logs
  244:     if (/^>\s*\S/.test(l)) continue;                                          // `> build · <model>` banner
  245:     if (/^[→▶]/.test(l)) continue;                                            // `→ Read <path>` tool traces
  246:     kept.push(l);
  247:   }
  248:   return kept.join('\n').trim();
  249: }
  250: 
  251: /**
  252:  * Read only the approved top-level identity pair from a successful terminal
  253:  * result. Do not inspect nested request echoes or completion stream events.
  254:  * @param {string} stdout
  255:  * @returns {{observedModels:string[],source:string,observedAt:string}|undefined}
  256:  */
  257: function extractOpencodeModelAttestation(stdout) {
  258:   const lines = String(stdout || '').split(/\r?\n/).map(stripOpencodeAnsi).filter((line) => line.trim());
  259:   for (let index = lines.length - 1; index >= 0; index--) {
  260:     let envelope;
  261:     try { envelope = JSON.parse(lines[index]); } catch (_) { continue; }
  262:     if (!isApprovedOpencodeTerminalEnvelope(envelope)) continue;
  263:     const provider = normalizeOpencodeIdentityComponent(envelope[OPENCODE_RUNTIME_MODEL_METADATA.providerField]);
  264:     const model = normalizeOpencodeIdentityComponent(envelope[OPENCODE_RUNTIME_MODEL_METADATA.modelField]);
  265:     if (!provider || !model) return undefined;
  266:     return {
  267:       observedModels: [`${provider}/${model}`],
  268:       source: OPENCODE_RUNTIME_MODEL_METADATA.source,
  269:       observedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
  270:     };
  271:   }
  272:   return undefined;
  273: }
  274: 
  275: function isApprovedOpencodeTerminalEnvelope(value) {
  276:   return value !== null && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value)
  277:     && OPENCODE_RUNTIME_MODEL_METADATA.schemaVersion === 1
  278:     && value.version === OPENCODE_RUNTIME_MODEL_METADATA.resultVersion

  280:     && value.subtype === OPENCODE_RUNTIME_MODEL_METADATA.terminal.subtype
  281:     && value.is_error === false;
  282: }

  285:   if (typeof value !== 'string') return null;
