   61: ## grok-models-succeeds-but-hopper-dispatch-auth-failed
   62: 
   63: ---
   64: 
   65: ## ISSUE: `grok models` 成功，但 `hopper-dispatch` 真实派发终止为 `adapter-auth-failed`
   66: 
   67: > 状态：待 Hopper 开发者调查（根因未定）
   68: >
   69: > 首次记录：2026-08-05
   70: > 严重性：高——已完成 OAuth 登录的同一 Grok CLI 可以取得 live model catalog，
   71: > 但 Hopper 的真实 read-only 派发在执行阶段失败，阻断唯一允许的外部审查路线。
   72: 
   73: ### 影响版本与环境
   74: 
   75: | 项目 | 已观测值 |
   76: |---|---|
   77: | 操作系统 | Windows |
   78: | App host chain | `pwsh.exe -> codex.exe -> ChatGPT.exe -> explorer.exe` |
   79: | 用户 / profile | `grok login --oauth`、`grok models` 与 Hopper 均在同一用户、同一 profile 语境中执行 |
   80: | Hopper | `hopper-dispatch` 0.49.1 |
   81: | Grok CLI | 0.2.118 |
   82: | Grok binary | 唯一发现的 binary：`C:\Users\litianyi\.grok\bin\grok.exe` |
   83: | 请求的 vendor / model / reasoning / sandbox | `grok` / `grok-4.5` / `high` / `read-only` |
   84: | Windows sandbox 强制性 | `unknown/unverified`；本文不把请求的 `read-only` 误报为已独立验证的实际隔离 |
   85: 
   86: ### 背景与期望
   87: 
   88: 用户已执行 `grok login --oauth`。在上述同一 App host chain 内：
   89: 
   90: 1. `grok models` 以 exit 0 完成。
   91: 2. `hopper-dispatch --setup grok --deep` 显示 `READY`；live catalog 为 1/1，模型为
   92:    `grok-4.5`，并且它报告的是同一 Grok binary。
   93: 3. OAuth artifact 仅确认**存在**；本记录不读取、复制或输出其内容。
   94: 
   95: 因此，至少应当能够区分「model catalog 可访问」与「真实 prompt 执行的认证 /
   96: 授权 / 协议问题」。如果真实派发失败，诊断应当准确、脱敏且可归因，而不能只让
   97: 已通过 catalog 探测的环境得到无可行动依据的 `adapter-auth-failed`。
   98: 
   99: 补充观察：`HOME` 与 `GROK_HOME` 为空时，CLI 会给出
  100: `auto worktree gc failed...` warning；但 `grok models` 仍以 exit 0 完成。该 warning
  101: 是待检验的影响因素，不是已确认根因。
  102: 
  103: ### 实际结果
  104: 
  105: 本次新建的独立任务为
  106: `T-HAWK-VENDOR-POLICY-GROK-ONLY-REVIEW-AUTHED-20260805`。它先 `resolve` 成功，随后以
  107: 下列精确命令派发：
  108: 
  109: ```text
  110: hopper-dispatch T-HAWK-VENDOR-POLICY-GROK-ONLY-REVIEW-AUTHED-20260805 --background --vendor grok --model grok-4.5 --reasoning high --sandbox read-only
  111: ```

  115: - 诊断为 `adapter-auth-failed`；`recovered=none`。

  133:

  148: 
  149: ### 可复现步骤
  150: 
  151: 在同一 Windows 用户 / profile / host chain 中进行；不要把 credential 内容贴入终端、
  152: issue 或日志。
  153: 
  154: 1. 交互式执行 `grok login --oauth`；只确认 OAuth artifact 存在，不读取其内容。
  155: 2. 执行 `grok models`，记录 exit code 与可见模型列表是否包含 `grok-4.5`。
  156: 3. 执行 `hopper-dispatch --setup grok --deep`，记录 `READY`、live catalog 及它解析的
  157:    binary 路径。
  158: 4. 准备一个符合项目协议的独立 task contract，执行
  159:    `hopper-dispatch --resolve <task-id> --vendor grok`，确认 resolve 成功。
  160: 5. 使用上文的精确 background dispatch 命令，随后通过 `--progress` 与
  161:    `--result --full` 记录终态、`recovered`、effective/observed model 与解析产物。
  162: 6. 作为等价的最小 direct 对照，用户待执行：
  163: 
  164:    ```text
  165:    grok -p "Reply exactly OK" --output-format json --no-auto-update -m grok-4.5 --cwd "F:\workspace\project\hawk-watcher" --permission-mode bypassPermissions --effort high
  166:    ```
  167: 
  168:    该 direct result 目前为 **pending，等待用户补充**；本 issue 不臆造其 exit code、
  169:    remote message 或输出。
  170: 
  171: ### 根因状态与当前假设（按证据强度排序）
  172: 
  173: 根因尚未确定。当前证据**不支持**把 ChatGPT App host、不同 user/profile 或 PATH
  174: 存在多个 Grok binary 作为首要解释：`grok models` 与 Hopper setup 均在所述同一 host
  175: 链和 profile 中运行，且只发现一个 binary。这不是对所有机器的一般性排除结论。
  176: 
  177: 1. **catalog endpoint 与 prompt endpoint 的 scope/session 差异。** `grok models` 的
  178:    成功只能证明 catalog 路径可用，不能证明 prompt endpoint 所需 OAuth scope、session
  179:    或 model entitlement 可用；这是现有观测直接保留的最高优先级假设。
  180: 2. **background runner 与 direct CLI 的环境、stdio 或 cwd 差异。** detached/background
  181:    spawn 可能没有继承 direct 调用需要的变量、stdin 语境、工作目录或 session 相关状态。
  182: 3. **adapter auth keyword classifier 的 false positive。** 当前 `adapter-auth-failed`
  183:    没有伴随可归因的 machine-readable remote code / 匹配规则 / 来源 stream；非认证错误
  184:    或模型正文中的关键词可能被过度归类。
  185: 4. **空 `HOME` / `GROK_HOME` 下的 `auto worktree gc failed...` warning。** 它与
  186:    `grok models` 成功并存，尚无证据表明它影响 prompt 执行，但应通过 direct/runner
  187:    环境对比验证。
  188: 
  189: ### 开发调查建议
  190: 
  191: - 在不泄露 secret 的前提下，为 auth classifier 记录触发它的 machine-readable code、
  192:   匹配规则标识与来源 stream（stdout / stderr / exit / parser）；将任何远端信息脱敏后
  193:   写入诊断。
  194: - 对比 direct 与 runner 的精确 argv、显式/继承环境、cwd、stdin/stdio、是否 detached，
  195:   并标记每项的观测来源；特别检查 `cli/src/vendors/grok.js` 到 subprocess 的边界。
  196: - 不要因为模型正文、prompt 回显或泛化的 `auth` 关键词就归类为认证失败；先区分认证、
  197:   endpoint scope、model entitlement、CLI protocol 与 parser failure。
  198: - 保持错误诊断无 token、cookie、OAuth artifact 内容或未脱敏的 remote payload。
  199: - 增加 fixture / tests，至少覆盖：真实 auth code、非 auth remote failure、含 auth 字样的
  200:   模型文本/回显、background 环境差异，以及无 parser-designated sidecar 的结果。
  201: 
  202: ### 验收标准
  203: 
  204: 1. 若上述 direct `grok -p` 成功，Hopper 使用等价 argv 时也应完成；否则必须给出准确的
  205:    **非 auth** 诊断和可验证的分类依据。
  206: 2. 若 direct 命令失败，Hopper 必须提供脱敏后的真实远端 code/message，而不是无依据地
  207:    泛称认证失败。
  208: 3. 任一路径不得输出 raw secret、OAuth artifact 内容或受保护原始日志内容。
  209: 4. 终态、`recovered` 与 requested/effective/observed model metadata 必须与实际运行
  210:    一致；Windows 上 sandbox enforcement 仍须标为 `unknown/unverified`，除非有独立证据。
  211: 
  212: ### 安全边界与非目标
  213: 
  214: - 不改变 Grok `read-only` 声明，不把其在 Windows 上的请求语义升级为未经验证的强制隔离。
  215: - 不绕过权限，不自动重新登录，不自动 retry，也不改派或换 vendor。
  216: - 本 issue 仅记录和定位诊断问题；不包含 credential、token 或原始日志内容。
  217: 
  218: ### 时间线与未决问题
  219: 
  220: | 时间 / 顺序 | 事件 | 状态 |
  221: |---|---|---|
  222: | 2026-08-05，派发前 | OAuth 已登录；`grok models` exit 0；`--setup grok --deep` 为 `READY`，catalog 1/1 `grok-4.5` | 已观测 |
  223: | 既有任务 | `T-HAWK-VENDOR-POLICY-GROK-ONLY-REVIEW-20260805` 终态为 `failed` / `adapter-auth-failed` / `recovered=none` | 已观测 |
  224: | 2026-08-05T13:29:52.639Z | 新的、用户显式授权的独立任务启动；PID 51648 | 已观测 |

  227: 
  228: 未决问题：direct 命令是否成功？若成功，runner 与 direct 的 argv/env/cwd/stdio 哪一项

  232: 
  233: ### 非重复性说明
  234: 
  235: `docs/archive/ISSUES.md` 已有与 Grok 相邻的历史记录：
  236: `grok-adapter-protocol-invalid-false-fail`、
  237: `grok-claude-buffered-output-idle-falsekill` 与
  238: `grok-model-line-rotation-stale-knownGood`。它们分别涉及结果解析、后台 idle watchdog
  239: 或模型 knownGood 轮换，均不是「同一 binary 的 `grok models` 成功而真实 Hopper 派发
  240: 被诊断为 `adapter-auth-failed`」这一未定根因；本记录因此不是重复 issue。

  250: ## ISSUE: codex adapter's `danger-full-access` bypass flag never reaches the spawned codex argv → still `workspace-write` → 1326 (extends ISSUE-codex-windows-sandbox-1326)

 succeeded in 1251ms:
  470:  * @param {import('./types.js').VendorAdapter} args.adapter
  471:  * @param {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts} [args.adapterOpts]
  472:  */
  473: export async function executeWithAdapter({ resolved, adapter, adapterOpts = {}, cwd = null, hopperDir = null }) {
  474:   const { task, vendor, composedPrompt } = resolved;
  475:   // Dispatch gate — the canonical sync spawn chokepoint (covers the CLI sync path AND any other
  476:   // caller, e.g. executeDispatch). A vendor disabled by capability (agy headless-output) is
  477:   // blocked here unless explicitly opted in. Throws before any subprocess is spawned.
  478:   assertVendorDispatchable(vendor);
  479:   const dispatchAdapterOpts = resolveAdapterOptsForTask(resolved, adapterOpts);
  480:   assertAdapterSandboxEnforceable(adapter, dispatchAdapterOpts);
  481:   // An explicit subject root is a forced process guard. Validate it before
  482:   // adapter preparation and before any vendor spawn; unsupported macOS backend
  483:   // or a non-read-only effective sandbox fail closed.
  484:   prepareSubjectRootGuard({
  485:     subjectRoot: dispatchAdapterOpts.subjectRoot,
  486:     sandbox: dispatchAdapterOpts.sandbox,
  487:   });
  488: 
  489:   // envPreflight — if not ok, fail FAST without spawning subprocess
  490:   const preflight = adapter.envPreflight();
  491:   if (!preflight.ok) {
  492:     return {
  493:       task,
  494:       vendor,
  495:       output: {
  496:         text: '',
  497:         status: 'auth-fail',
  498:         error: `Adapter ${vendor} preflight failed: ${preflight.missing.join(' | ')}`,
  499:       },
  500:       raw: { exitCode: -1, stdout: '', stderr: '', timedOut: false, durationMs: 0 },
  501:     };
  502:   }
  503: 
  504:   // Prepare log file if adapter wants one (codex F2 silent-fail detection)
  505:   let logPath = null;
  506:   if (typeof adapter.prepareLog === 'function') {
  507:     const hint = adapter.prepareLog(task.id, adapter.name);
  508:     logPath = hint.logPath || null;
  509:   }
  510: 
  511:   // Build args (adapter may want logFile threaded through opts).
  512:   // Phase 6c F1: include task.taskType so timeoutMs can apply review-task floor.
  513:   // Thread the resolved vendor CWD through opts so adapters that take a
  514:   // working-dir flag (e.g. opencode --dir) can pass it explicitly.
  515:   const effectiveOpts = { ...dispatchAdapterOpts, logFile: logPath, taskType: task.taskType, cwd: cwd || undefined };
  516: 
  517:   // Spawn subprocess ONCE (per spec §3 #4).
  518:   // Phase 6c F2: resolve adapter.command with deterministic known-install
  519:   // paths (NOT vendor-retry orchestration) so installers that don't add
  520:   // their bin to PATH (agy on Windows) still work. Resolved FIRST so size-gated
  521:   // prompt delivery knows the OS command-line regime (cmd.exe shim vs native .exe).
  522:   const resolvedCmd = resolveCommandWithKnownPaths(adapter.command, adapter.knownInstallPaths || []);
  523:   const spawnCommand = resolvedCmd ? resolvedCmd.command : adapter.command;
  524:   const prependArgs = resolvedCmd ? resolvedCmd.prependArgs : [];
  525: 
  526:   // Size-gated prompt delivery (ISSUE-codex-bypass-flag-missing-from-argv): inline
  527:   // small prompts; for an over-budget command line write handoffs/<task>-prompt.md
  528:   // and pass the vendor a small "read this file" pointer. Needs hopperDir to locate
  529:   // handoffs/; without it (e.g. direct-injected E2E adapters) fall back to inline.
  530:   let args;
  531:   let delivery = null;
  532:   if (hopperDir) {
  533:     delivery = resolvePromptDelivery({
  534:       adapter, composedPrompt, opts: effectiveOpts,
  535:       resolvedCmd: spawnCommand, prependArgs,
  536:       handoffsDir: join(hopperDir, 'handoffs'), taskId: task.id,
  537:     });
  538:     if (delivery.fallbackReason) {
  539:       // Pointer delivery was wanted but unusable → INLINE with bytes > budget (the
  540:       // silent-no-op risk class). Surface it instead of falling back quietly.
  541:       process.stderr.write(`hopper: WARNING prompt-file delivery fell back to INLINE — ${delivery.fallbackReason}. Command line ${delivery.bytes}B (budget ${delivery.budget}B, ${delivery.regime}); may be truncated on Windows cmd.exe.\n`);
  542:     }
  543:     args = delivery.args;
  544:   } else {
  545:     args = adapter.args(composedPrompt, effectiveOpts);
  546:   }
  547:   const spawnArgs = prependArgs.length > 0 ? [...prependArgs, ...args] : args;
  548: 
  549:   // STDIN delivery (win-cmd-shim): the delivery layer routes the full prompt to stdin
  550:   // (adapter emitted a sentinel in argv). Prefer it over the static stdinMode check.
  551:   const stdinInput = (delivery && delivery.channel === 'stdin' && delivery.stdinPrompt != null)
  552:     ? delivery.stdinPrompt
  553:     : (adapter.stdinMode === 'pipe' ? composedPrompt : null);
  554:   // HOPPER-3: optional adapter env (e.g. codex CODEX_HOME auto-isolation).
  555:   const adapterEnv = typeof adapter.env === 'function' ? adapter.env(effectiveOpts) : undefined;
  556:   // 乙: idle + ceiling instead of a single total cap. The per-vendor
  557:   // adapter.timeoutMs() now seeds the ceiling (floored to ≥30 min); idle (silence)
  558:   // is the real "stuck" detector. --timeout / HOPPER_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MS override
  559:   // the ceiling; HOPPER_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS overrides idle.
  560:   const { idleMs, ceilingMs } = resolveDispatchTimeouts(adapter.timeoutMs(effectiveOpts), effectiveOpts);
  561:   const raw = await runSubprocessOnce({
  562:     command: spawnCommand,
  563:     args: spawnArgs,
  564:     stdinInput,
  565:     timeoutMs: ceilingMs,
  566:     idleMs,
  567:     logFilePath: logPath,
  568:     vendorName: adapter.name,
  569:     cwd: cwd || undefined,
  570:     env: adapterEnv,
  571:     subjectRoot: dispatchAdapterOpts.subjectRoot,
  572:     sandbox: dispatchAdapterOpts.sandbox,
  573:   });
  574: 
  575:   // Parse result (adapter-specific failure classification)
  576:   const output = adapter.parseResult(raw);
  577: 
  578:   return { task, vendor, output, raw };
  579: }
  580: 
  250:   };
  251: 
  252:   try {
  253:     releaseVendorLock = await acquireVendorLock(adapter.name);
  254:   } catch (err) {
  255:     fail(outputMdPath, `Could not acquire vendor lock: ${err.message}`, { taskId, hopperDir, adapterName });
  256:   }
  257: 
  258:   // *** THE single spawn (spec §3 #4) ***
  259:   // Single spawn() call. Windows command resolution above is pure-sync
  260:   // PATH lookup (no subprocess, no execSync).
  261:   //
  262:   // Per codex Phase 5 F1 recheck (POSIX side): vendor MUST be detached so it
  263:   // gets its own process group; otherwise killProcessTree(-pid) hits the
  264:   // runner's group, not the vendor's children. Windows uses taskkill /T
  265:   // which works without detached. detached on POSIX puts vendor in new pgrp.
  266:   const isWindows = process.platform === 'win32';
  267:   const vendor = spawn(invocation.command, invocation.args, {
  268:     // Vendor stdio is always piped through this parent. Passing it the log file
  269:     // descriptors would let an inherited FD write inside subjectRoot despite the
  270:     // sandbox rule. The runner tees both streams to the same log without reducing
  271:     // visibility, while its own inherited stdout/stderr may still target the log.
  272:     stdio: [promptStdinFile ? 'pipe' : 'ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  273:     windowsHide: true,
  274:     // Retro #3 fix: vendor runs in the repo root that owns .hopper/ (passed via
  275:     // --cwd by spawnDetached), not the runner's own process.cwd(). Falls back to
  276:     // process.cwd() only if --cwd was not supplied (older callers).
  277:     cwd: vendorCwd || process.cwd(),
  278:     // HOPPER-3: merge optional adapter env (e.g. codex CODEX_HOME auto-isolation)
  279:     // over the inherited environment.
  280:     env: { ...process.env, ...(typeof adapter.env === 'function' ? adapter.env(adapterOpts) : {}) },
  281:     detached: !isWindows,
  282:   });
  283: 
  284:   const teeToLog = (fd) => (chunk) => {
  285:     try { writeSync(fd, chunk); } catch (_) { /* final log write is best-effort */ }
  286:   };
  287:   vendor.stdout?.on('data', teeToLog(fdOut));
  288:   vendor.stderr?.on('data', teeToLog(fdErr));
  289: 
  290:   // STDIN delivery — observable contract (NOT best-effort): write the full prompt,
  291:   // end() (EOF — load-bearing: codex reads stdin to completion, openai/codex#20919),
  292:   // and record any short-write / write error so the exit handler can mark the dispatch
  293:   // `prompt-delivery-fail` rather than silently treating a truncated/empty prompt as a
  294:   // real run. The `stdin.on('error')` swallows EPIPE (vendor died mid-write) but RECORDS
  295:   // it — it never crashes the runner, and it never hides the failure.

 succeeded in 1052ms:
Grok Build TUI

Usage: grok [OPTIONS] [PROMPT] [COMMAND]

Arguments:
  [PROMPT]
          Initial prompt for the interactive session, e.g. `grok "fix the bug"` or `grok --worktree=feat "create this feature"`

Options:
      --agent <NAME>
          Agent name or definition file path

      --agents <JSON>
          Inline subagent definitions as JSON

      --allow <RULE>
          Permission allow rule (compat alias: --allowedTools)

      --always-approve
          Auto-approve all tool executions

  -c, --continue
          Continue the most recent session for the current working directory

      --cwd <CWD>
          Working directory

      --debug
          Enable debug logging

      --debug-file <FILE>
          Write debug logs to FILE

      --deny <RULE>
          Permission deny rule (compat alias: --disallowedTools)

      --disable-web-search
          Disable web search and web fetch tools

      --disallowed-tools <TOOLS>
          Built-in tools to remove (comma-separated)

      --experimental-memory
          Enable cross-session memory

      --fork-session
          When resuming (`--resume` / `--continue`), create a new session ID instead of reusing the original (optionally set via `--session-id`)

      --fullscreen
          Open in the standard fullscreen TUI for this session, overriding a config `[ui] screen_mode = "minimal"` preference. Session-scoped only — does not write config. Fullscreen-vs-inline still follows the alt-screen policy (--no-alt-screen, [terminal] alt_screen, terminal auto-detection)

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

      --include-partial-messages
          Emit incremental `stream_event` lines (text/thinking deltas) alongside whole messages. Only affects `--output-format streaming-messages-json`

      --json-schema <SCHEMA>
          JSON Schema for structured output. When set, the model is constrained to produce JSON matching this schema. Implies --output-format json. Example: --json-schema '{"type":"object","properties":{"name":{"type":"string"}}}'

      --leader-socket <PATH>
          Use a custom leader socket path instead of the default `~/.grok/leader.sock`

  -m, --model <MODEL>
          Model ID to use

      --max-turns <N>
          Maximum number of agent turns

      --minimal
          Experimental: scrollback-native rendering. Finalized blocks are printed into the terminal's native scrollback (use the terminal's own scroll / selection); a small pinned region holds the prompt + running turn. Session-scoped only — does not write config. To default plain `grok` to minimal, set `[ui] screen_mode = "minimal"` in ~/.grok/config.toml

      --no-alt-screen
          Run inline instead of using the terminal alternate screen

      --no-memory
          Disable cross-session memory for this session

      --no-plan
          Disable plan mode

      --no-subagents
          Disable subagent spawning

      --oauth
          Use OAuth when the welcome screen starts authentication

      --output-format <OUTPUT_FORMAT>
          Output format for headless mode

          Possible values:
          - plain
          - json
          - streaming-json:          NDJSON of the agent native ACP session updates
          - streaming-messages-json: NDJSON in the Anthropic Messages API wire format
          
          [default: plain]

  -p, --single <PROMPT>
          Single-turn prompt. Prints the response to stdout and exits

      --permission-mode <MODE>
          Permission mode
          
          [possible values: default, acceptEdits, auto, dontAsk, bypassPermissions, plan]

      --prompt-file <PATH>
          Single-turn prompt from a file

      --prompt-json <JSON>
          Single-turn prompt as JSON content blocks

  -r, --resume [<SESSION_ID_OR_TITLE>]
          Resume a session by ID or title, or the most recent if omitted. Non-ID values match session titles for the current directory (ignoring letter case; a sole renamed match wins among duplicates, otherwise ambiguity errors; UUID-shaped values always mean IDs)

      --reasoning-effort <EFFORT>
          Reasoning effort for reasoning models
          
          [aliases: --effort]

      --restore-code
          Check out the original session's commit when resuming

      --rules <RULES>
          Extra rules to append to the system prompt

  -s, --session-id <SESSION_ID>
          Use a specific session UUID for a **new** conversation (must be a valid UUID and must not already exist under the target session directory). With `--resume`/`--continue`, only valid together with `--fork-session` (names the forked session). Does not resume existing sessions — use `--resume` / `--continue` instead

      --sandbox <PROFILE>
          Sandbox profile for filesystem and network access
          
          [env: GROK_SANDBOX=]

      --system-prompt-override <PROMPT>
          Override the agent's system prompt (compat alias: --system-prompt)

      --tools <TOOLS>
          Built-in tools to allow (comma-separated)

  -v, --version
          Print version

      --verbatim
          Send the prompt exactly as given

  -w, --worktree [<WORKTREE>]
          Start the session in a new git worktree, optionally named

      --worktree-ref <WORKTREE_REF>
          Branch, tag, or commit to base the worktree on (with `--worktree`). Defaults to the current HEAD of the source checkout when omitted
          
          [aliases: --ref]

AROUND 122699296
Coming from Claude, Cursor, or Codex?your settings, rules & skills carry over# Coming from Claude, Cursor, or Codex?
 your settings, rules, and skills come with you. Grok Build
reads the same project conventions other agents use, and imports the rest.
- **Rules & instructions** 
 `AGENTS.md` (the Codex/OpenCode convention),
  `CLAUDE.md` (including nested ones), and `*.md` rules under
  `.claude/rules/` and `.cursor/rules/`.
- **Skills & custom commands** 
 `~/.claude/skills/`, `~/.claude/commands/`,
  `~/.cursor/skills/`, and their project-level twins. Flat command `.md`
 from `~/.claude.json`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, and project
- **Hooks** 
 from `.claude/settings.json`, including matcher aliases like
  `Bash`, so most hooks run unchanged.
**`/import-claude`** scans your `~/.claude` settings 
vars, MCP servers, hooks 
skills continue a recent session from those tools right here.
Run **`grok inspect`** in a repo to see every rules file, skill, and MCP
server Grok picked up, tagged with where it came from. Each compat source
can be toggled in `[compat.claude]` / `[compat.cursor]` config sections.
*Go deeper: `/docs Project Rules (AGENTS.md)`, `/docs Skills`, or `/docs MCP Servers`*

paths = ["~/my-team-skills"]          # additional directories to scan

### Harness compatibility
Control vendor compatibility for Cursor, Claude, and Codex. Every cell defaults to `true`. Session cells stay staged and inert until a foreign-session scanner consumes them, and each tool needs both its `sessions` cell and the matching `resume-claude`, `resume-codex`, or `resume-cursor` skill 
[compat.cursor]
skills = true     # scan ~/.cursor/skills/ and <cwd>/.cursor/skills/
rules = true      # scan ~/.cursor/rules/ and <dir>/.cursor/rules/
agents = true     # scan ~/.cursor/ for named instruction files
mcps = true       # scan ~/.cursor/mcp.json and <cwd>/.cursor/mcp.json
hooks = true      # scan ~/.cursor/hooks.json and <cwd>/.cursor/hooks.json
sessions = true   # staged; no scanner consumer yet
[compat.claude]
skills = true     # scan ~/.claude/skills/ and <cwd>/.claude/skills/
rules = true      # scan ~/.claude/rules/ and <dir>/.claude/rules/
agents = true     # scan ~/.claude/ and <dir>/.claude/CLAUDE*.md
mcps = true       # scan ~/.claude.json for MCP servers
hooks = true      # scan ~/.claude/settings.json for hooks
sessions = true   # staged; no scanner consumer yet
[compat.codex]
sessions = true   # staged; no scanner consumer yet
Codex's `skills`, `rules`, `agents`, `mcps`, and `hooks` cells are reserved and currently inert 
For Claude and Cursor, `rules` and `agents` are independent: turning off named instruction files doesn't disable the home or project rules directory, and turning off rules doesn't disable named files. Claude's `agents` cell gates home-level `~/.claude/` named files and project `<dir>/.claude/CLAUDE*.md`; generic top-level `Claude.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `CLAUDE.local.md` stay recognized. Project rule paths are scanned at every directory from the repo root down to the current one.
`grok inspect` reports cells that still need session-start resolution as `?` until a value is available; cells with an explicit env or TOML value use that value. Affected discovery entries report `compatibilityStatus: "unresolved"` in JSON and `[compat unresolved]` in human output.
AROUND 123086785
12-project-rules.mdPer-directory instructions and precedence rules# Project Rules (AGENTS.md)
Project rules let you configure Grok per project or directory. By placing an AGENTS.md file in your repository, you can set coding conventions, build instructions, style guides, and any other instructions that Grok should follow when working in that codebase.
## What Are Project Rules?
Project rules are Markdown files that Grok reads and adds to its context. Grok follows their content for every interaction in that tree.
- `Agents.md`
- `AGENTS.md`
Grok loads every matching file in a directory, so a folder that contains both `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` contributes both. On case-insensitive filesystems, names that resolve to the same file (such as `Agents.md` and `AGENTS.md`) are deduplicated and counted once. `Claude.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `CLAUDE.local.md` are supported for compatibility with Claude Code workflows. When Claude compatibility is enabled (the default), Grok also scans your home-level `~/.claude/` directory for these filenames and, at each directory level, checks `.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/CLAUDE.local.md` -- the locations Claude Code uses for project memory. With Cursor compatibility enabled, the home-level `~/.cursor/` directory is scanned the same way.
### Rules Directories
In addition to AGENTS.md files, Grok scans for `*.md` files in rules directories at each level (`<dir>`) from the repo root to the current working directory:
| `<dir>/.grok/rules/` | Always scanned |
| `<dir>/.claude/rules/` | Claude compatibility (configurable) |
| `<dir>/.cursor/rules/` | Cursor compatibility (configurable) |
Grok also scans home-level rules, regardless of where it starts. These roots are already vendor-specific, so rules live directly under `rules/`:
| `$GROK_HOME/rules/` (default `~/.grok/rules/`) | Always scanned; applies to all projects |
| `~/.claude/rules/` | Controlled by `compat.claude.rules` |
| `~/.cursor/rules/` | Controlled by `compat.cursor.rules` |
Home rules load first, in the table order, followed by project files from repo root to the current directory. Files are alphabetical within each rules directory. The vendor `rules` cells control both home and project rules independently of the corresponding `agents` cells. Claude's `agents` cell controls named files under `~/.claude/` and project `<dir>/.claude/CLAUDE*.md`; generic top-level names such as `Claude.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `CLAUDE.local.md` remain recognized. See [Configuration](05-configuration.md#harness-compatibility).
Grok scans for project rules in this order:
1. **Home rules**: `$GROK_HOME`, then enabled `~/.claude/` and `~/.cursor/` sources
2. **Repo rules**: If inside a git repo, every directory from the repo root down to the current working directory (inclusive)
  AGENTS.md              # "Use TypeScript. Follow ESLint rules."
    AGENTS.md            # "Prefer functional components."
      AGENTS.md          # "Use CSS modules for styling."
Grok orders the files from the repo root to the current working directory, so files in deeper directories appear later in its context and take precedence when instructions conflict. In the example above, if the root says "Use styled-components" but `components/AGENTS.md` says "Use CSS modules", the CSS modules instruction wins because it appears later.
## What to Put in Project Rules
- Prefer functional components with hooks over class components
## Scoping Rules to Subdirectories
AGENTS.md files scope to the entire directory tree rooted at their folder. Use this to provide different instructions for different parts of your codebase:
  AGENTS.md                    # Monorepo-wide rules
      AGENTS.md                # "Use React. Prefer CSS modules."
      AGENTS.md                # "Use Express. Follow REST conventions."
      AGENTS.md                # "No framework-specific code in this package."
## Session Rules Flags
To add rules for a single session without editing files, pass `--rules` (alias `--append-system-prompt`):
grok --rules "Always use TypeScript. Prefer functional components."
To replace the system prompt entirely, pass `--system-prompt-override` (alias `--system-prompt`). Grok uses the text verbatim and skips both the default system prompt and `--rules`. (Text passed with `--rules`, by contrast, is wrapped in a `<human_rules>` block and appended to the default prompt.)
Grok loads each project instruction file in full; there is no character cap and no truncation. Even so, keep instructions concise and focused. Shorter, specific rules are easier for Grok to follow than long ones, and every file you load consumes context.

  258: ## [0.47.0] - 2026-08-05
  259: 
  260: 一次外部事故报告的排查，牵出四个互相遮蔽的缺陷。它们放在一起才解释得通：
  261: 每一个都让另一个更难被发现。
  262: 
  263: ### Fixed — `--setup` 的大半份报告是 dead code，包括唯一的配置漂移 lint
  264: 
  265: `runSetup()` 里有一个无条件 `return;`（`03330ea`，2026-07-22 引入），挡在整份报告
  266: 之前。已登记为 [`setup-sandbox-column-dead-code`](docs/archive/ISSUES.md#setup-sandbox-column-dead-code)（2026-07-29），但当时把范围
  267: 记成了「Sandbox 一列」。**实际被挡掉的是**：Runtime/Workspace 块、verdict、vendors
  268: 表（含 Sandbox 列）、Auth notes、`--deep` 的两个 drift 段、Task-type policy lint、
  269: Next steps。
  270: 
  271: 其中 Task-type policy lint 的丢失代价最大：它是**唯一**会报告 `.hopper/AGENTS.md`
  272: 有没有 batch-2 机器解析列（`Effort policy` / `Model rule`）的界面。它死掉之后，
  273: batch 2 之前 scaffold 的项目没有任何信号说自己需要迁移。实测发现一个外部项目仍停在
  274: **v0.28.0** 的 scaffold（落后 18 版），`--model` fallback 链第二级整条失效，
  275: 现场把症状误判成了「`--swarm` 不传 model」。**本插件自己的 dogfood workspace
  276: 也有同样的漂移**，且 `code-impl` / `sidecar-polish` 指向未获批准的 vendor
  277: （自 v0.40.0 fail-closed 起必然被拒），一并修正。
  278: 
  279: `03330ea` 合法引入的闭合 inventory 投影与 grok auth-context 边界**保留**，
  280: 折进完整报告而非替换它。新增测试逐段断言，任何位置的下一个提前 return 都会失败。
  281: 
  282: ### Fixed — 经软链调用时静默 exit 0、零输出
  283: 
  284: 直接执行守卫比较的是 `resolve(process.argv[1])` 与 realpath 过的 `import.meta.url`。
  285: `path.resolve()` 不跟随软链/junction，所以经 npm-global 软链调用时两者永不相等，
  286: `main()` 根本不跑——**exit 0，零输出**，对自动化调用方而言与成功无法区分。
  287: 
  288: 修复同时关掉第二条同样后果的路径：**Windows 上 `realpathSync` 不规范化大小写**
  289: （实证：`F:/workspace/…` 与 `F:/WORKSPACE/…` 都能解析且结果不字符串相等），
  290: 任何交出不同大小写 argv[1] 的启动器都会重新制造同一个故障。现在两侧都 realpath、
  291: 都各自 try/catch（此前 `realpathSync(__filename)` 无保护，抛出即模块顶层未捕获异常），
  292: win32 折叠大小写而 POSIX 不折叠。判定逻辑抽成 `isDirectInvocation()` 并单测。
  293: 
  294: 守卫必须在「否」方向保持严格——`cli/bin/hopper-dispatch` 被测试 import
  295: （`parseProbeCacheRecoveryArgs`），误判为真会让 `main()` 在测试进程里跑起来。已加断言。
  296: 
  297: ### Fixed — `binary_availability` / `binary_basename` 是硬编码的占位符
  298: 
  299: `cli/src/setup.js` 在探针缓存缺失时传入字面量 `'unknown'` / `null`，于是每台机器上
  300: `--setup` 与每份 handoff frontmatter 的这两个字段永远是 unknown。
  301: `installCheckForAdapter` 早就知道答案，只是没人问。现改为真实观测。
  302: 
  303: ### Added — `--binaries`：一个 vendor 名在 PATH 上究竟解析到几个文件
  304: 
  305: 事故根因不是模型也不是配置，是**一台机器上装了两份 codex**：hopper spawn 到
  306: `~/bin/codex` 的 0.131.0，而用户 PowerShell 解析到 nvm4w 的 0.146.0。派发因此对
  307: 需要 ≥0.144 的模型报 400，症状看上去像账号或能力问题。hopper 此前无法观测到这件事——
  308: 它只解析 PATH 的第一个命中。
  309: 
  310: `hopper-dispatch --binaries [<vendor>] [--deep]` 列出每一个命中、dispatch 实际
  311: spawn 的是哪个、以及（`--deep`）各自版本；版本分歧会升级为 `--setup` 的 Next steps 一条。
  312: 枚举是纯 fs（默认层即可用），版本查询每个文件一次 spawn（仅 `--deep`），
  313: 沿用 `vendor-compat.js` 的 spawn 隔离约定。
  314: 
  315: **绝对路径只出现在 `--binaries`。** `--setup` / `--check` / `--models` /
  316: `--capabilities` 是 public discovery 面，按 `model-attestation-contract.test.js`
  317: 的契约不得输出本地路径——`--setup` 给的是无路径摘要（计数、裁决、已解析版本号）。
  318: `--binaries` 放在 `.hopper/` 工作区闸之上：机器上装了什么是机器的属性，不是队列的。
  319: 
  320: ### Changed — 仓库身份统一，并打了第一个 tag
  321: 
  322: `git remote` 仍指向重命名前的用户名，而三份元数据已是 `litianyi-007`。GitHub 的
  323: 重命名重定向掩盖了它，但旧用户名可被他人重新注册。已统一。同时打上首个 tag
  324: `v0.46.0`——此前 0 个 tag，上游版本发现没有可依赖的锚点。
  325: 
  326: ## [0.46.0] - 2026-08-03
  327: 
  328: ### Changed — license is now MIT, and the LICENSE file finally contains a license
  329: 
  330: All three sibling plugins (harnessloop, hopper, kata) were unified on MIT. MIT
  331: asks the least of downstream: keep the notice, and that is all. Apache-2.0 also
  332: requires shipping a LICENSE copy, preserving NOTICE, and **stating your changes**
  333: — the last of which is the obligation people most often violate without noticing.
  334: 
  335: **Stated plainly, because it is a real trade-off:** MIT carries no express patent
  336: grant and no patent-retaliation clause. For enterprise adopters Apache-2.0's
  337: patent grant is arguably the friendlier half. That protection was given up
  338: deliberately, not overlooked.
  339: 
  340: **The change applies from this release onward. v0.45.2 and earlier remain under
  341: Apache-2.0 — that cannot be undone; do not read the whole history as MIT.**
  342: 
  343: ### Fixed — the LICENSE file was a stub, and had been since the beginning
  344: 
  345: `LICENSE` was **19 lines**: the Apache *file-header* boilerplate (the block meant
  346: to go at the top of a source file) plus a "Full license text: <url>" line. The
  347: entire TERMS AND CONDITIONS body — sections 1 through 9 — was absent. Meanwhile
  348: `package.json`, five other manifests and three README badges all declared
  349: `Apache-2.0`. `gh repo view --json licenseInfo` returned **`Other`**: GitHub could
  350: not identify it. Anyone relying on this repo's license was reading a claim with
  351: no terms behind it.
  352: 
  353: Nothing caught it because everything that existed — `version consistency`,
  354: `release metadata` — guards *declared fields*. Not one check ever opened the
  355: LICENSE file. **The declaration was verified; the fact was not.**
  356: 
  357: ### Added
  358: 
  359: - `tests/unit/license-integrity.test.js`. Asserts `LICENSE` carries the
  360:   substantive MIT text (grant sentence, `without restriction`, the AS-IS
  361:   disclaimer) — existence and a title line both pass for a stub, which is exactly
  362:   how the stub survived. Then it walks every `*.json` in the repo, collecting
  363:   every `license` value at any nesting depth, and requires them all to equal
  364:   `package.json`'s — discovery-based, so a new manifest is covered without
  365:   touching a list. `package-lock.json` is deliberately narrowed to its own root
  366:   entry: dependencies' licenses are third-party facts, and six of them are still
  367:   Apache-2.0. Rewriting those would be fabricating provenance, not relicensing.
  368: 
  369:   Destructive proof: restoring the old 19-line stub → red on both tests; flipping
  370:   one manifest to `Apache-2.0` → red, naming the file and path.
  371: 
  372: npm test 1140 / 1138 pass / 0 fail / 2 skipped; integration 53 / 52 pass / 0 fail.
  373: 
  374: ## [0.45.2] - 2026-08-03
  375: 
  376: ### Fixed
  377: 
  378: - **`setVendorCache` touched the shared cache file outside its own lock.** The
  379:   function opened `readCacheWithOutcome()` unconditionally at the top, before
  380:   `acquireLock()` — its result was needed by exactly one rare branch (the
  381:   parent-owner-only hardening failure), but it ran on every call. That read could
  382:   land while another process, holding the lock, was mid-`renameSync` over the same
  383:   destination. On POSIX a concurrent reader never disturbs a rename; on Windows
  384:   the `MoveFileEx`-based replace can raise a transient sharing violation
  385:   (EBUSY/EPERM) when another handle holds the destination open. The read is now
  386:   inside the one branch that needs it, so **every touch of the shared file happens
  387:   under the lock**.
  388: 
  389:   No retry, no sleep, no timeout change — those would have made the light green
  390:   without making the check hold. Atomicity is unchanged (write-to-temp + atomic

  452:     && adapter?.liveness?.safeStreamEvents === false;
  453:   const emitsProcessAliveLiveness = bufferedOutput || adapter?.liveness?.processAlive === true;
  454:   bufferedLivenessTimer = emitsProcessAliveLiveness ? setInterval(
  455:     appendBufferedLiveness,
  456:     bufferedLivenessIntervalMs,
  457:   ) : null;
  458:   if (bufferedLivenessTimer && bufferedLivenessTimer.unref) bufferedLivenessTimer.unref();
  459:   // idle poll: reset the silence clock whenever the log grows; kill after idleMs
  460:   // of no growth. Poll cadence is a fraction of idleMs (clamped 1–5s).
  461:   //
  462:   // Heartbeat-aware variant: an adapter that emits an idle heartbeat to its log
  463:   // (adapter.idleHeartbeatRe — mimo's --print-logs /session/status poll) would keep the size
  464:   // growing forever and defeat the detector, so for those we reset the clock only on SUBSTANTIVE
  465:   // growth (a newly-appended non-heartbeat line). This reaps a mimo background process that
  466:   // delivered its answer but never exits (session-status hang); mimo.parseResult then classifies
  467:   // the idle reap as success and extracts the already-captured answer from the log.
  468:   const heartbeatRe = adapter && adapter.idleHeartbeatRe ? adapter.idleHeartbeatRe : null;
  469:   let lastSize = -1;
  470:   let lastReadOffset = 0;          // heartbeat-aware path: bytes already scanned for substance
  471:   let lastGrowAt = Date.now();
  472:   const pollMs = Math.max(1000, Math.min(idleMs || 0, 5000));
  473:   idlePoll = (idleMs > 0 && !bufferedOutput) ? setInterval(() => {
  474:     let sz = 0;
  475:     try { sz = existsSync(logPath) ? statSync(logPath).size : 0; } catch (_) {}
  476:         if (sz !== lastSize) {
  477:           lastSize = sz;
  478:           if (contentFreeProcessLiveness) {
  479:             // This adapter has no safe lifecycle envelope. File growth still
  480:             // resets the idle clock, but raw bytes are never opened or parsed.
  481:             lastReadOffset = sz;
  482:             lastGrowAt = Date.now();
  483:             return;
  484:           }
  485:           if (sz > lastReadOffset) {
  486:             let chunk = null;
  487:             try {
  488:               const fd = openSync(logPath, 'r');
  489:               try {
  490:                 const len = sz - lastReadOffset;
  491:                 const buf = Buffer.allocUnsafe(len);
  492:                 const n = readSync(fd, buf, 0, len, lastReadOffset);
  493:                 chunk = buf.toString('utf8', 0, n);
  494:               } finally { closeSync(fd); }
  495:             } catch (_) { chunk = null; }
  496:             if (chunk === null) {
  497:               // Read failed this tick (I/O hiccup): be conservative — DON'T reap on a read error,
  498:               // and DON'T advance the offset, so the appended bytes are re-scanned next poll (no skip).
  499:               lastGrowAt = Date.now();
  500:               return;
  501:             }
  502:             lastReadOffset = sz;
  503:             appendVendorHeartbeat(chunk);
  504:             if (!heartbeatRe || chunkHasSubstantiveLine(chunk, heartbeatRe)) { lastGrowAt = Date.now(); return; }
  505:             // Only a declared heartbeat grew → fall through to the idle check.
  506:           } else {
  507:             // Initial empty log or a size shrink/rotation: resync and count it as activity once.
  508:             lastReadOffset = sz; lastGrowAt = Date.now(); return;
  509:           }
  510:         }
  511:     if (Date.now() - lastGrowAt >= idleMs) { clearInterval(idlePoll); killVendor('idle'); }
  512:   }, pollMs) : null;
  513:   if (idlePoll && idlePoll.unref) idlePoll.unref();
  514: 
  515:   // `exit` can arrive before the child stdio handles have closed. Read the
  516:   // file-backed stream only on `close`, after EOF, so final completion records
  517:   // cannot be lost to the exit/read race. appendRunnerTerminalEvent remains
  518:   // idempotent if another terminal writer has already marked the task.
  519:   vendor.on('close', (code, signal) => {
  520:     releaseVendorLockOnce();

codex
A broader control-flow issue is now evident: the background runner deliberately reads one interleaved log and passes the same full string as both `stdout` and `stderr` (`cli/bin/hopper-runner:535–541`). That turns every adapter’s “stderr-only” matcher into a whole-transcript matcher and is the simpler common cause behind several neighboring false-classification risks. The claimed 0.47.0 precedent is historically inaccurate: that release did not touch any vendor adapter.

  111:       // extracted text), that reap is graceful — classify it success with the delivered answer,
  112:       // NOT a failure. A genuine timeout (ceiling budget, or idle with no completed answer) stays
  113:       // a timeout. (timeoutReason is threaded by hopper-runner; absent on the sync path.)
  114:       const log = raw.logFileContent || raw.stdout || '';
  115:       const parsed = extractMimoJsonText(log);
  116:       // Only a TERMINAL step_finish (part.reason !== 'tool-calls' — i.e. the model actually
  117:       // stopped generating: 'stop'/'end_turn'/'length'/…) means the answer finished. A
  118:       // 'tool-calls' step_finish is just a mid-turn tool boundary, so gating on "any step_finish"
  119:       // would let a mid-task idle reap be mislabeled success with a PARTIAL answer (codex review).
  120:       const completed = mimoAnswerCompleted(log);
  121:       if (raw.timeoutReason === 'idle' && completed && parsed.text) {
  122:         return {
  123:           text: parsed.text,
  124:           status: 'success',
  125:           usage: parsed.totalTokens ? { totalTokens: parsed.totalTokens } : undefined,
  126:         };
  127:       }
  128:       return { text: parsed.text || raw.stdout, status: 'timeout', error: `mimo run timed out after ${raw.durationMs}ms` };
  129:     }
  130:     if (raw.exitCode === 127 || /not found|command not found/i.test(raw.stderr || '')) {
  131:       return {

  133:         status: 'permission-fail',

  135:       };

  171: /**
  172:  * True iff the mimo JSON stream contains a TERMINAL step_finish — one whose `part.reason` is a
  173:  * generation-stop ('stop' / 'end_turn' / 'length' / 'completed' / …), NOT a 'tool-calls' step
  174:  * boundary (which only means "ran a tool, another turn is coming"). Distinguishes a completed
  175:  * answer from a mid-task idle reap, so parseResult classifies an idle reap as success ONLY when
  176:  * the answer actually finished. Exported for tests. Coupled to mimo's `--format json` event
  177:  * schema (reason at part.reason); revisit on mimo upgrades (see adapter staleAfter).
  178:  * @param {string} log  full mimo stdout/log
  179:  * @returns {boolean}
  180:  */
  181: export function mimoAnswerCompleted(log) {
  182:   if (!log) return false;
  183:   for (const line of String(log).split(/\r?\n/)) {
  184:     const t = line.trim();
  185:     if (!t.includes('"step_finish"')) continue;
  186:     let ev;
  187:     try { ev = JSON.parse(t); } catch (_) { continue; }
  188:     if (!ev || ev.type !== 'step_finish') continue;
  189:     const reason = String(ev.part?.reason ?? ev.reason ?? '').toLowerCase();
  190:     if (reason !== 'tool-calls' && reason !== 'tool_calls' && reason !== 'tool_use') return true;
  191:   }
  192:   return false;
  193: }
  194:

  197:   if (!trimmed) return { text: '', totalTokens: undefined };
  198: 
  199:   const lines = trimmed.split(/\r?\n/).filter((line) => line.trim());
  200:   const chunks = [];
  201:   let parsedJson = false;
  202:   let totalTokens;
  203: 
  204:   for (const line of lines) {
  205:     try {

  149:       return { text: raw.stdout, status: 'timeout', error: `copilot -p timed out after ${raw.durationMs}ms` };
  150:     }

  153:         text: '',

  155:         error: 'copilot binary not found. Install: npm install -g @github/copilot OR brew install copilot-cli',
  156:       };
  157:     }
  158:     // Auth failure backstop: envPreflight's profile heuristic can't tell a logged-OUT
  159:     // copilot (stale ~/.copilot session-store) from a logged-in one, so catch a real
  160:     // auth failure here and label it (don't bury it in the generic unknown-fail bucket).
  161:     const authFail = /not (authenticated|logged in|signed in)|unauthorized|401|authentication (failed|required)|please (sign in|log in|authenticate)|run `?copilot`? to (sign in|log in)|invalid (token|credentials)/i;

  163:       return {

  166:         error: 'copilot is not authenticated. Run `copilot` once to log in, or set GH_TOKEN/GITHUB_TOKEN/COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN.',
  167:       };
  168:     }

  170:     if (raw.stderr.match(/quota|rate.limit|premium request/i) || raw.stdout.match(/quota exceeded|rate.limit/i)) {
  171:       return {
  172:         text: raw.stdout,
  173:         status: 'permission-fail',
  174:         error: 'Copilot premium request quota exceeded. Upgrade subscription or wait for monthly reset.',
  175:       };
  176:     }

  178:       // Strip Copilot footer: "Changes / Requests / Tokens" usage block
  179:       let text = raw.stdout;
  180:       // Try to find and trim the usage footer (starts at "Changes" or "Requests" near end)
  181:       const footerStart = text.search(/\n\s*(Changes|Requests)\s+[+\-]?\d/);
  182:       if (footerStart > 0) text = text.slice(0, footerStart);
  183:       // Parse usage from footer if present
  184:       const usageMatch = raw.stdout.match(/Requests\s+([\d.]+)\s+Premium/);
  185:       const usage = usageMatch ? { premiumRequests: parseFloat(usageMatch[1]) } : undefined;
  186:       return { text: text.trim(), status: 'success', usage };
  187:     }
  188:     return {
  189:       text: raw.stdout,
  190:       status: 'unknown-fail',
  191:       error: `copilot exited ${raw.exitCode}: ${(raw.stderr || '').slice(0, 500)}`,
  192:     };

  166:   parseResult(raw) {
  167:     // Per codex Phase 2 audit F2: classify over BOTH log + stderr.
  168:     // Missing log is distinguishable from empty-clean log.
  169:     if (raw.timedOut) {
  170:       return { text: raw.stdout, status: 'timeout', error: `agy -p timed out after ${raw.durationMs}ms (default 5min print-timeout exceeded)` };
  171:     }
  172:     if (raw.exitCode === 127) {
  173:       return {
  174:         text: '',
  175:         status: 'permission-fail',
  176:         error: 'agy binary not found in PATH. Run `agy install` to configure PATH.',
  177:       };
  178:     }
  179: 
  180:     // Combine signals: log file content (if read) + stderr (always captured).
  181:     // Per F2: stderr may contain failure pattern when log is missing/disabled.
  182:     const log = raw.logFileContent || '';
  183:     const logFileMissing = raw.logFileContent === undefined;
  184:     const signal = `${log}\n${raw.stderr || ''}`;
  185:     // In BACKGROUND, the runner captures stdout+stderr into one log and passes it as raw.stdout,
  186:     // so raw.stdout is the whole log (agy's Go-klog diagnostics + any answer). Strip the glog to
  187:     // recover the actual ANSWER; an answer-less run (only glog) must NOT count as stdout (else a
  188:     // headless run that emitted no answer false-succeeds — agy 1.0.12 `--print` drops the answer
  189:     // to a non-TTY stdout entirely, see the empty-stdout branch below).
  190:     const answerText = stripAgyGlog(raw.stdout);
  191:     const hasStdout = Boolean(answerText);
  192: 
  193:     // Auth classification (V5 fix — agy false "never auth"). agy print mode emits transient
  194:     // "not logged in" / "Failed to get OAuth token" noise during boot, then recovers ~0.3s
  195:     // later via silent KEYRING auth, emitting an AUTHORITATIVE success marker. The old
  196:     // heuristic matched the boot noise even AFTER recovery (and the empty stdout from the
  197:     // non-TTY drop) → FALSE auth-fail although the run authed + completed. Fix = a
  198:     // success-marker VETO: if a terminal auth-success marker is present, agy authed, so never
  199:     // classify auth-fail regardless of early noise or empty stdout. A genuine failure shows
  200:     // the noise (incl. "silent auth failed" / "keyringAuth timed out") WITHOUT a success marker.
  201:     // Anchor the keyring marker to its real emitter ("ChainedAuth: authenticated via keyring
  202:     // (effective: keyring)") so a NEGATED phrase (e.g. "could not be authenticated via keyring")
  203:     // can't falsely veto a genuine auth failure. The other two markers are already specific.
  204:     // NOTE: the failure branches below match substrings over the COMBINED log+stderr `signal`,
  205:     // which in background also holds glog from sub-calls. They are gated on `!hasStdout` so a run
  206:     // that produced a real answer is NEVER misrouted to a failure by incidental sub-call noise
  207:     // (e.g. a tool that hit a deadline / permission error mid-run, then recovered and answered).
  208:     const authSucceeded = /Print mode: silent auth succeeded|OAuth: authenticated successfully|ChainedAuth:[^\n]*authenticated via keyring/i.test(signal);
  209:     const authNoise = /You are not logged into Antigravity|Failed to get OAuth token|error getting token source|Print mode: silent auth failed|keyringAuth: timed out/i.test(signal);
  210:     if (authNoise && !authSucceeded && !hasStdout) {
  211:       return {
  212:         text: '',
  213:         status: 'auth-fail',
  214:         error: `agy is not OAuth-authed (no silent-auth success marker in this run). Run \`agy\` interactively once (browser OAuth) to populate the keyring.${logFileMissing ? ' [Note: --log-file content was missing; matched stderr]' : ''}`,
  215:       };
  216:     }
  217: 
  218:     if (!hasStdout && /deadline exceeded|context cancelled|context deadline/i.test(signal)) {
  219:       return {
  220:         text: raw.stdout,
  221:         status: 'timeout',
  222:         error: 'agy print-timeout. Increase via --print-timeout OR check network.',
  223:       };
  224:     }
  225: 
  226:     if (!hasStdout && /permission denied|permission error|access denied|forbidden|not allowed/i.test(signal)) {
  227:       return {
  228:         text: raw.stdout,
  229:         status: 'permission-fail',
  230:         error: `agy permission error. Signal excerpt: ${signal.slice(0, 300)}`,
  231:       };
  232:     }
  233: 
  234:     if (raw.exitCode === 0 && hasStdout) {
  235:       return { text: answerText, status: 'success' };
  236:     }
  237: 
  238:     // Exit 0 + NO answer text (after stripping glog). agy 1.0.12 `--print` renders the answer
  239:     // ONLY in its interactive TUI — under a non-TTY stdout (every headless dispatch) it emits
  240:     // nothing capturable: the answer is NOT on stdout/stderr and NOT in the --log-file (verified
  241:     // 2026-06-25 on 1.0.12; regressed from the earlier non-TTY behaviour). A PTY wrap would
  242:     // capture it but node-pty is excluded for agy by project policy (agy hangs on an open stdin
  243:     // pipe). So this is a real OUTPUT limitation, surfaced as a failure — NEVER a false success.
  244:     if (raw.exitCode === 0 && !hasStdout) {
  245:       if (authSucceeded) {
  246:         return {
  247:           text: '',
  248:           status: 'unknown-fail',
  249:           error: `agy authed + ran to completion but emitted NO answer text on a non-TTY stdout — agy 1.0.12 \`--print\` only renders the answer in its interactive TUI (the answer is not on stdout/stderr or in --log-file). Headless answer capture needs a PTY, which is excluded for agy (open-stdin hang). Treat agy as interactive-only until this is resolved upstream.`,
  250:         };
  251:       }
  252:       return {
  253:         text: '',
  254:         status: 'unknown-fail',
  255:         error: `agy returned no answer text with exit 0 but no matching error pattern.${logFileMissing ? ' [Note: --log-file content was missing — adapter could not read diagnostic log.]' : ''} Log excerpt: ${log.slice(0, 300)}. Stderr excerpt: ${(raw.stderr || '').slice(0, 200)}`,
  256:       };
  257:     }
  258: 
  259:     return {
  260:       text: raw.stdout,
  261:       status: 'unknown-fail',
  262:       error: `agy exited ${raw.exitCode}.${logFileMissing ? ' [log file missing]' : ''} Stderr: ${(raw.stderr || '').slice(0, 300)}. Log: ${log.slice(0, 300)}`,
  263:     };
  264:   },

docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1529-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1530-### Root cause (symptom proven; precise mechanism partially characterized)
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1531-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1532:**What is empirically proven:** on this Windows host, a multi-line composed
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1533-brief delivered as an argv positional to opencode (a cmd.exe `.cmd` shim at
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1534-`C:\nvm4w\nodejs\opencode.cmd`) arrives **cut to its first segment** — the
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1535-vendor consistently receives only the header/preamble and asks for the rest.
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1536:The failure is 100% reproducible for multi-line briefs and absent for
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1537-single-line ones.
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1538-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1539-**What is NOT precisely established** (wording corrected after independent
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1540-audit): the exact cutting mechanism. An earlier version of this ISSUE stated
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1541:flatly that "cmd.exe truncates a multi-line argv positional at the first
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1542-newline at any size." That is too strong: the pointer instruction shipped in
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1543:the original fix was itself multi-line and DID reach the vendor intact
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1544-(opencode read the prompt file and completed the task), so "cmd.exe
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1545:truncates any multi-line argv at the first newline" cannot be the whole
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1546-story. The precise mechanism — cmd.exe line-length/quoting interaction,
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1547-Node's win32 argv escaping through a `.cmd` shim, or opencode's own argv
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1548-parsing of embedded newlines — is left to a follow-up repro experiment
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1549:(`cmd.exe /c opencode.cmd run "<controlled multi-line string>"` with varying
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1550-lengths/quoting). What matters operationally is unchanged: **argv-inline
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1551:delivery of multi-line briefs to cmd-shim vendors is unreliable**, so the
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1552-pointer-file channel is the correct fix regardless of which exact layer cuts
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1553-the text.
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1554-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1555-Confirmed contributing facts:
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1556-
--

docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1584-- stdin-capable vendors (codex/claude/mimo, opt-in copilot) still use the
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1585-  stdin channel on cmd-shim (checked before the new gate).
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1586-- Single-line prompts within budget still inline (deterministic path).
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1587:- native-exe and POSIX regimes are untouched (their argv is multi-line-safe).
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1588-- The cwd-scope guard and write-failure fallback (fall back to inline with
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1589-  `fallbackReason`, never break dispatch) apply to the forced pointer exactly

docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1595:- multi-line on native-exe/posix → still inline;

docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1598-  argv, `run …` argv shape preserved.

docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1604-#### Audit follow-up (2026-07-24, pointer hardening)
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1605-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1606-An independent audit (approve-with-comments) noted that the ORIGINAL
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1607-`buildPointerInstruction` itself produced a 4-line pointer — inconsistent with
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1608:this ISSUE's own "multi-line argv is at risk" theory (and, per the corrected
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1609-root-cause wording above, evidence that the precise cutting mechanism is not
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1610-fully characterized). Hardening shipped in the follow-up commit:
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1611-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1612-- `buildPointerInstruction` now emits a **single line** with the prompt-file
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1613-  absolute path **front-loaded**: even if some layer ever cuts an argv
--
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1720-  `.cmd`/`.bat` cmd.exe shim **and** the adapter cannot read stdin
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1721-  (`adapter.promptStdin !== 'supported'`) **and** the prompt contains any
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1722-  newline — **regardless of size**.
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1723-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1724-`composePrompt()` (`cli/src/tasks.js:143-154`) always joins sections with
docs/archive/ISSUES.md:1725:`'\n\n---\n\n'`, so every real prompt is multi-line. Kimi
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1726-(`cli/src/vendors/kimi.js:32`, `stdinMode: 'none'`) is exactly the non-stdin
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1727-case. Net effect: on Windows, **essentially every dispatch to a non-stdin vendor
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1728-writes one of these**, not just oversized ones.
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1729-
docs/archive/ISSUES.md-1730-#### 2. Nothing ever deletes them

# Adversarial review

## Summary

The immediate diagnosis is substantially correct: both top-level Grok tasks completed successfully, but Hopper failed to extract their envelopes and then mislabeled the parser failure as authentication failure. The analysis nevertheless conflates this incident with a separate configuration-isolation concern, misses the background runner’s destruction of stdout/stderr provenance, and contains several historical and numerical inaccuracies. Severity profile: three P1 design/correctness findings and two P2 evidence-quality findings.

Assumption: “run succeeded” means the top-level Grok task completed, not that every optional MCP/plugin initialized successfully.

## Files reviewed

| Files | Scope reviewed |
|---|---:|
| [grok.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/grok.js) | Full file, 323 LOC |
| [hopper-runner](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/bin/hopper-runner:250), [subprocess.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/subprocess.js:190), [dispatch.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/dispatch.js:473) | Approximately 640 relevant LOC |
| [codex.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/codex.js:17), [claude.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/claude.js:112), [kimi.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/kimi.js:122), [opencode.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/opencode.js:135), `mimo.js`, `copilot.js`, `agy.js` | Approximately 810 relevant LOC |
| [validation.js](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/validation.js:267), [CHANGELOG.md](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/CHANGELOG.md:258), [ISSUES.md](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/docs/archive/ISSUES.md:59), Grok parser tests | Approximately 650 relevant LOC |
| [AUTHED raw log](F:/workspace/project/hawk-watcher/.hopper/handoffs/T-HAWK-VENDOR-POLICY-GROK-ONLY-REVIEW-AUTHED-20260805-output.log), [first raw log](F:/workspace/project/hawk-watcher/.hopper/handoffs/T-HAWK-VENDOR-POLICY-GROK-ONLY-REVIEW-20260805-output.log) | Full files: 545 lines each; 170,523 and 172,615 bytes |
| Both sanitized output files | Full files: 75 lines each |

## Per-claim verdict

| Claim | Verdict | Evidence checked |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | **CONFIRMED**, with scope qualification | AUTHED envelope begins at byte **149,693** / character **149,691**, not byte 149,691. It parses to `text.length=16854`, `stopReason="end_turn"`, `num_turns=5`, `output_tokens=7978`, `total_cost_usd=0.318382`; process exit was 0. The first run’s envelope begins at byte **149,707** / character **149,705**, with 15,013 text characters and 7,612 output tokens. Both contain complete task answers. However, `end_turn` does coexist with genuine optional-MCP auth failures: both logs report `hawk-agent` and `stitch` as `Auth required` at lines 504–505. Those are not xAI/Grok account-auth failures and did not prevent completion. |
| C2 | **CONFIRMED** | Candidate order is whole stdout at [grok.js:280](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/grok.js:280), last nonempty line at 283–288, then first-`{`/last-`}` framing at 290–303. The first brace is character 145,948 / byte 145,950 inside `ParseFile {…}`; the real envelope starts later. The resulting slice is invalid JSON. Fixing only envelope extraction would make these exact runs succeed because the success return at lines 206–210 precedes auth classification at line 212. Fixing only the auth regex would leave `adapter-protocol-invalid`. |
| C3 | **CONFIRMED** | The optional group makes bare `invalid` match; optional `HTTP` makes bare 401 or 403 match. Each raw log has exactly **two** matches, both `invalid matcher groups…`: once in the warning and once in its `ParseFile` detail at lines 498–499. No numeric 401/403 match caused this incident. Because the background runner supplies the same log as stdout and stderr, the constructed `signal` contains four effective occurrences. |
| C4 | **OVERSTATED** | The family characterization—classification based on unstructured mixed transcript text—is sound, but the claimed 0.47.0 precedent is historically wrong. Commit `b652a07`/v0.47.0 touched no vendor adapter, and its changelog covers setup, entry-guard, and binary-discovery work. The `not found` matcher remains present in Grok, Claude, Kimi, and MiMo today. C2 is also framing-by-delimiter rather than a substring classifier, though it shares the lost-structure root problem. |
| C5 | **CONFIRMED** | A direct `grok inspect --json` from `C:\Windows\Temp`, after removing all `HOPPER_*`, `CLAUDE_*`, and `CODEX_*` variables, still loaded `C:\Users\litianyi\.claude\settings.json`, 238 skills, and 22 Hopper-attributed skills. Grok’s runtime inspection reports Claude/Cursor compatibility cells enabled by default, including Cursor hooks. Hopper passes cwd and inherited environment but contains no code that explicitly loads these global files. Cwd can add project-level rules, but it does not explain the global paths: they loaded from a neutral cwd too. |
| C6 | **OVERSTATED** | Only Codex defines `env()` at [codex.js:407](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/codex.js:407), but that does not mean it is the only adapter with isolation controls: Claude has opt-in `--bare` at [claude.js:127](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/claude.js:127), while Grok natively exposes `[compat.claude]` and `[compat.cursor]` cells. The behavioral gap—Hopper does not disable Grok’s foreign-harness surfaces—is real. The reported counts are inaccurate: current `grok inspect` reports **138 loaded and 11 skipped**, not 276/22; it does report 238 skills and 22 Hopper-attributed entries. The stale winner is confirmed, although its metadata disagrees internally: `package.json` says 0.32.0 while `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` says 0.31.0; the losing cache is 0.48.1. |
| C7 | **OVERSTATED** | The recorded false `adapter-auth-failed` is definitively a Hopper correctness defect, not bad Grok account usage. But “not a usage problem” is too absolute: malformed Cursor hooks, stale plugins, broken MCP executables, and two optional MCP auth failures are genuine user-profile problems and are documented inputs to Grok’s default compatibility system. They are counterfactual triggers/workaround targets, while Hopper’s parser and classifier remain the root cause of the false terminal state. |

## Findings

[F1] P1: Background execution destroys stream provenance and duplicates every diagnostic

Root cause: [hopper-runner:535–541](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/bin/hopper-runner:535) reads one interleaved log and assigns the entire string to both `stdout` and `stderr`. Consequently, supposedly stderr-only classifiers inspect assistant prose, stdout envelopes, runner notices, and startup warnings twice.

Recommended fix: Tee into the public combined log while separately retaining stdout and stderr for `parseResult`. At minimum, attach stream provenance to framed records and never pass a combined transcript as both streams.

[F2] P1: The proposed isolation-first order would mask, not fix, this incident

Root cause: Removing the malformed Cursor warning would remove the particular early brace and bare `invalid`, making these fixtures pass accidentally. Any future warning containing `{`, `invalid`, `401`, or `403` could reproduce the failure.

Recommended fix: Repair stream provenance and structural envelope extraction first; then encode authoritative-envelope precedence and narrow the textual classifiers. Treat compatibility isolation as separate hardening.

[F3] P1: The neighboring matcher family is broader than the analysis reports

Root cause: Whole-stream classifiers remain at:

- [grok.js:198,316](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/grok.js:198)
- [claude.js:190,204,211](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/claude.js:190)
- [kimi.js:126,132,137](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/kimi.js:126)
- `mimo.js:130,139,146`
- `copilot.js:161–170`
- [codex.js:465–468](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/codex.js:465)

OpenCode is the favorable counterexample: it uses per-line structured events and has no equivalent auth/binary substring branch. Agy also uses whole-stream patterns, but gates failures on absence of recovered answer text and has an explicit auth-success veto.

Recommended fix: Require structural/vendor codes where available; otherwise gate matchers on nonzero exit, absence of authoritative completion, and the correct source stream.

[F4] P2: `GROK_HOME` alone is not a viable isolation mechanism

Root cause: With `GROK_HOME=C:\Windows\Temp`, direct inspection still loaded 138 Claude permission rules and all 22 Hopper-attributed skills; Claude/Cursor compatibility remained enabled. Grok’s own embedded documentation explicitly says home rules are `$GROK_HOME`, followed by enabled `~/.claude` and `~/.cursor` sources.

Recommended fix: Use an isolated Grok home only together with `[compat.claude]` and `[compat.cursor]` surfaces disabled, while preserving the required Grok authentication artifact. Do not describe a plain `GROK_HOME` override as hermetic.

[F5] P2: The real envelope exposes another unreported schema drift

Root cause: Both current envelopes use `stopReason: "end_turn"`, while [grok.js:310](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/grok.js:310) recognizes only exact `"EndTurn"` as verified completion. After fixing extraction, the run would be successful but incorrectly marked `unknown-completeness`.

Recommended fix: Normalize case and separators before comparing terminal reasons, and add fixtures for the observed lowercase `end_turn`. Existing tests use only capitalized `EndTurn` and omit a pre-envelope warning containing braces.

## Anything missed / simpler explanation

The simplest complete explanation is:

1. The background runner collapses stdout and stderr into one transcript and supplies it twice.
2. Grok emits a valid pretty-printed result after approximately 149 KB of compatibility/startup diagnostics.
3. The framing parser assumes no prior brace, so it misses the result.
4. The auth regex then sees bare `invalid` in a Cursor hook parse warning and changes what should be `adapter-protocol-invalid` into `adapter-auth-failed`.

Environment isolation is not required to explain any part of the false terminal state.

The logs also contain issues absent from the original list: optional MCP auth failures for `hawk-agent` and `stitch`, repeated `fable` handshake failures, and hundreds of skill-discovery warnings. They did not prevent completion, but they demonstrate why whole-transcript classification is untenable and may contribute materially to startup time/context cost.

## Single most likely way the analysis is wrong

It treats Grok compatibility isolation as part of the incident’s causal fix. Compatibility contamination is real hardening work, but it neither caused Hopper’s incorrect interpretation nor makes the parser safe; putting it first would merely remove today’s triggering warning.

## Fix order

The proposed order `(a) isolation → (b) heuristics → (c) structural rule` is not right.

Recommended order:

1. Preserve stdout/stderr separately and fix structural envelope extraction. Parse the final top-level object from the correct stream using a brace/string-aware decoder or reverse candidate scan—not first-`{`/last-`}`.
2. Add the authoritative terminal rule and regression fixtures. Scope it to a parser-designated Grok result with exit 0, nonempty text, no envelope error, and a recognized successful terminal reason.
3. Narrow Defect B and audit every neighboring matcher listed above.
4. Add Grok compatibility isolation as separate deterministic-execution hardening.

The structural rule must still permit independent infrastructure failures—timeout, prompt-delivery failure, or sandbox/subject-guard violation—to override vendor output. “Any parseable `end_turn` substring can never be overridden” would itself be unsafe.

## Verdict

**PASS_WITH_CHANGES**

The immediate RCA is correct, but its causal framing, historical precedent, isolation mechanism, and fix order should be revised before implementation.

**Commit:** N/A — read-only review. Source snapshot: `1f30922`.

**Checks:** No files were modified by this review. Before and after, the workspace already contained modified `docs/archive/ISSUES.md` and the dispatcher-managed untracked `.hopper/handoffs/grokauth-rootcause-review-20260805-output.md`; `git diff --name-only` remained `docs/archive/ISSUES.md`.

**Next recommendation:** create `T-GROK-PARSER-STREAM-PROVENANCE-REWORK`, covering runner stream separation, Grok envelope parsing, terminal-reason normalization, and cross-adapter matcher fixtures.

- [claude.js:190,204,211](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/claude.js:190)
- [kimi.js:126,132,137](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/kimi.js:126)
- `mimo.js:130,139,146`
- `copilot.js:161–170`
- [codex.js:465–468](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/codex.js:465)

OpenCode is the favorable counterexample: it uses per-line structured events and has no equivalent auth/binary substring branch. Agy also uses whole-stream patterns, but gates failures on absence of recovered answer text and has an explicit auth-success veto.

Recommended fix: Require structural/vendor codes where available; otherwise gate matchers on nonzero exit, absence of authoritative completion, and the correct source stream.

[F4] P2: `GROK_HOME` alone is not a viable isolation mechanism

Root cause: With `GROK_HOME=C:\Windows\Temp`, direct inspection still loaded 138 Claude permission rules and all 22 Hopper-attributed skills; Claude/Cursor compatibility remained enabled. Grok’s own embedded documentation explicitly says home rules are `$GROK_HOME`, followed by enabled `~/.claude` and `~/.cursor` sources.

Recommended fix: Use an isolated Grok home only together with `[compat.claude]` and `[compat.cursor]` surfaces disabled, while preserving the required Grok authentication artifact. Do not describe a plain `GROK_HOME` override as hermetic.

[F5] P2: The real envelope exposes another unreported schema drift

Root cause: Both current envelopes use `stopReason: "end_turn"`, while [grok.js:310](F:/workspace/ai/hopper-plugin/cli/src/vendors/grok.js:310) recognizes only exact `"EndTurn"` as verified completion. After fixing extraction, the run would be successful but incorrectly marked `unknown-completeness`.

Recommended fix: Normalize case and separators before comparing terminal reasons, and add fixtures for the observed lowercase `end_turn`. Existing tests use only capitalized `EndTurn` and omit a pre-envelope warning containing braces.

## Anything missed / simpler explanation

The simplest complete explanation is:

1. The background runner collapses stdout and stderr into one transcript and supplies it twice.
2. Grok emits a valid pretty-printed result after approximately 149 KB of compatibility/startup diagnostics.
3. The framing parser assumes no prior brace, so it misses the result.
4. The auth regex then sees bare `invalid` in a Cursor hook parse warning and changes what should be `adapter-protocol-invalid` into `adapter-auth-failed`.

Environment isolation is not required to explain any part of the false terminal state.

The logs also contain issues absent from the original list: optional MCP auth failures for `hawk-agent` and `stitch`, repeated `fable` handshake failures, and hundreds of skill-discovery warnings. They did not prevent completion, but they demonstrate why whole-transcript classification is untenable and may contribute materially to startup time/context cost.

## Single most likely way the analysis is wrong

It treats Grok compatibility isolation as part of the incident’s causal fix. Compatibility contamination is real hardening work, but it neither caused Hopper’s incorrect interpretation nor makes the parser safe; putting it first would merely remove today’s triggering warning.

## Fix order

The proposed order `(a) isolation → (b) heuristics → (c) structural rule` is not right.

Recommended order:

1. Preserve stdout/stderr separately and fix structural envelope extraction. Parse the final top-level object from the correct stream using a brace/string-aware decoder or reverse candidate scan—not first-`{`/last-`}`.
2. Add the authoritative terminal rule and regression fixtures. Scope it to a parser-designated Grok result with exit 0, nonempty text, no envelope error, and a recognized successful terminal reason.
3. Narrow Defect B and audit every neighboring matcher listed above.
4. Add Grok compatibility isolation as separate deterministic-execution hardening.

The structural rule must still permit independent infrastructure failures—timeout, prompt-delivery failure, or sandbox/subject-guard violation—to override vendor output. “Any parseable `end_turn` substring can never be overridden” would itself be unsafe.

## Verdict

**PASS_WITH_CHANGES**

The immediate RCA is correct, but its causal framing, historical precedent, isolation mechanism, and fix order should be revised before implementation.
